# Master Plan — project-wide gap register

Every recovered requirement that is **not** VERIFIED COMPLETE. Each gap keeps a stable ID
and traces back to its requirement row in `MASTER-PLAN-REQUIREMENT-TRACEABILITY.md`.

> **A CLOSED GAP KEEPS ITS ROW.** The document's practice has always been to retain a resolved
> gap with its closure note rather than delete it, so the history of what was wrong and what fixed
> it stays attached to the ID. Such a row carries `VERIFIED COMPLETE`. The sentence above
> describes what gets a row in the first place, not what stays.

> **THE REGISTER HAS TWO TABLE SHAPES AND ONLY ONE OF THEM HAS A STATUS COLUMN.** The original
> **185** rows are `| gap | requirement | phase | status | what remains | next action |`. The
> **94** rows added since — the 18 recovered on 2026-11-16 (`GAP-182`…`GAP-199`), the 31 raised
> by later work (`GAP-200`…`GAP-230`), 2 more untracked requirements found on 2026-11-24
> (`GAP-231`…`GAP-232`), **5 raised on 2026-11-25 by the independent review of the GAP-131 fix**
> (`GAP-233`…`GAP-237`), **2 more the same day from the 13-agent census of the storefront API**
> (`GAP-238`…`GAP-239`), **2 found while FIXING those** (`GAP-240`…`GAP-241`), **4 raised the
> same day by the independent review of the GAP-233/234/235 fix** (`GAP-242`…`GAP-245`), **1
> raised while closing the last of the GAP-142 families and WITHDRAWN the same day as false**
> (`GAP-246`), **2 carried out of the same batch's reviews** (`GAP-247`…`GAP-248`), **4 from closing the last
> GAP-142 families and the reviews of that batch** (`GAP-249`…`GAP-252`), and **1 from the
> independent financial review of the platform-revenue slice** (`GAP-253`), and **1 reported from
> outside its own permitted files while closing P4-F033's UI half** (`GAP-254`), and **1 from the
> independent review of the H2 change** (`GAP-255`), and **1 measured in a browser while scoping
> the product-card action rule** (`GAP-256`), and **2 raised by the independent review of the
> settled-order payment fix** (`GAP-257`…`GAP-258`), and **1 measured by the bilingual cart and
> checkout harness** (`GAP-259`), and **2 raised by the independent review of the refund-door stock
> fix** (`GAP-260`…`GAP-261`), and **1 measured while seeding P4-8's shipping and tax fixture**
> (`GAP-262`), and **1 found by the gateway classification census** (`GAP-263`), and **1 measured while reading the integration run's one Feature failure, which turned out to be a blind fixture rather than the guard it accused** (`GAP-264`), and **3 produced by building the Phase 01 `P01-G1`/`P01-G2` journey exercises, none of which any unit test had found** (`GAP-265`…`GAP-267`), and **1 measured by the ratchet written for the smallest of those three, which turned one broken route into 73** (`GAP-268`), and **4 measured by LOOKING at the first Flutter pixels this project has ever rendered** (`GAP-269`…`GAP-272`), and **2 from the Phase 03 storefront work — one measured on the one button its own row was not about, and one obligation of the recovered plan that had never been tracked at all** (`GAP-273`…`GAP-274`), and **1 created and closed the same day, by the independent review of the official-site editor** (`GAP-275`) — are
> `| gap | decision id(s) | phase | measured | next action |`: they record what was MEASURED in
> this container, and carry no status term. **279 rows in total.** The status counts below
> therefore describe the 185, and say nothing about the other 94. That is a real limitation, not a
> rounding: progress against the later rows cannot be counted mechanically, only read.

> **TWO OF THE FOUR (`GAP-242`, `GAP-243`) WERE FIXED IN THE SAME CHANGE THAT RAISED THEM** and
> carry `VERIFIED COMPLETE` in their text. Recorded as rows anyway rather than fixed silently: both
> were live, reachable, destructive defects that the suite did not cover, and the pattern that found
> them — reviewing a fix and then sweeping the codebase for the SAME STATEMENT elsewhere — is the
> thing worth keeping, not just the two patches.

> **A category-A gap must never be silently downgraded to C.** If one moves, the move needs
> an owner decision recorded against the gap ID.

> **Category assignment is mine, not the owner's.** It is derived from each row's status,
> gap text and next action — stated so nobody reads it as an owner classification.


## Totals

**181 open gaps** out of 350 recovered requirements, **plus 18 recovered on 2026-11-16** when the
owner supplied the Master Product Plan — requirements that were never tracked at all, in any
status, because their text was unavailable. **199 in total.**

The original 181 are unchanged: not renumbered, not recategorised, none closed. The 18 are held in
their own category **R** below precisely so that the two sets stay distinguishable.

| `GAP-060` | `P02-I4` | 02 Merchant Experi | PARTIALLY IMPLEMENTED | **SCOPE MATERIALLY ADVANCED 2026-11-26; two named surfaces remain open.** `x-ss.choice-group` / `x-ss.field-help` coverage goes from **5 product views to 16**: orders (`createOrder` payment type and status, `view`'s note type, `refund_details`' delivery-charge checkbox), shipping (`shipping_method/edit`, `free_shipping`, `shippingzone/create/edit`), staff permissions (`roles/create/edit`, the whole `permissions[]` grid as ONE named fieldset) and coupons (`create/edit`, a radio group that previously had **no group name of any kind** -- no legend, no label, not even an empty one). The two component patterns were matched rather than a third invented: a fieldset with legend for a SET of controls, `field-help` with `aria-describedby` for ONE, because a `<select>` is one control in the accessibility tree and wrapping a lone select in a fieldset is the WRONG association, not a stricter one. Three instruction claims were verified in code before being written: `to_customer` notes really are read back and shown on the shopper's own order page while `private_note` is not; the refund checkbox really POSTs on the click; and `free_shipping`'s options 3/4/5 really reveal the minimum-amount row. One sentence was deliberately NOT written -- that reports count a paid order -- because `ReportController` has no `payment_status` predicate and it would have been false. 10 en/ar pairs added as matched pairs (en 4700 -> 4710, ar 4694 -> 4704, both verified) and `ArabicUiCoverageTest::SURFACES` widened by five globs, measured at 117 keys and **0** missing before widening, so it is a strict strengthening that turns nothing red. **TWO DEFECTS CLOSED ON THE WAY.** (1) Arabic `'Manual'` was `'دليل'` -- a handbook -- on a radio where the word means BY HAND; now `'يدوي'`, and all four call sites are this one group so the fix is total. (2) The host's dark modal keeps a WHITE surface (`style-dark.css` sets `--bs-modal-bg: #ffffff`), on which `.ss-choicegroup__legend` measured **1.21:1** and `.ss-field__hint` **2.30:1**; P02-I4 is what made this reachable at scale, because contextual help had only ever been on PAGE screens (6.18:1 there) and six of this row's screens are modal partials. Fixed by naming both on that surface in `custom.css` AND `rtl-custom.css` -- mirrored files, so a rule in one reaches half the merchants -- now 16.88:1 and 6.42:1, which also closes the same latent defect for every pre-existing hint or legend in any modal. Verified in a real Chromium across the four configurations the merchant layout actually produces (the admin's dark mode is a STYLESHEET SWAP, not `prefers-color-scheme`, so emulating a colour scheme yields two identical light pages) at two widths: **152 contrast checks, 0 failed**, 11 `aria-describedby` references resolved, 0 orphans, 0 dangling, 0 overflow at 375px, and every instruction's inline-START edge matching its container -- the RTL check that cannot be faked, since a stuck-LTR paragraph matches on the left instead. **24 mutants, 24 caught**; one initially survived and was reported rather than papered over, because `disabled` on the component is a NON-mutant (it declares its props and never spreads `$attributes`), so the detector is pinned by a positive control instead. The harness produced **two false readings before correction**, both recorded in the committed scripts: loading 2 stylesheets where the layout loads 19+, and omitting the `.modal` wrapper on which Bootstrap declares `--bs-modal-bg`, which read the labels at 1.09:1 against the wrong backdrop. **THE RECONCILIATION THIS ROW ASKED FOR, DONE 2026-11-26, AND THE ANSWER IS "PARTLY".** Censused across all 31 views in `resources/views/setting/`: **radio groups 6 of 6 accounted for** (4 instructed plus 2 declared-uninstructed with named defects), **single selects 0 of 22 instructed** -- `x-ss.field-help` had ZERO call sites anywhere under `setting/` -- and 11 fieldsets of which 6 own a radio group, leaving **5 switch/checkbox groups** with a legend and no instruction. So the GROUP half was finished and the SINGLE-CONTROL half had not been started. **`SettingsChoiceGroupGuidanceTest` is green and correct and COULD NOT have said so: its `INSTRUCTED` constant is a declared list of four, not a census**, so it is green by construction for what it names and silent about everything it does not -- narrower than this row was reading it as, rather than wrong. That verdict is now executable rather than prose: `DeferredChoiceGuidanceTest` censuses the tree and requires every choice control to be instructed, declared broken, or declared OPEN with a reason, with the remainder pinned at an exact **26 items**; a cross-check fails if the two files ever disagree, and a mutation adding a new settings select is CAUGHT. **ALL THREE DEFERRED SURFACES ARE NOW INSTRUCTED, each sentence written from the code rather than the label.** (a) `shop_price`, chosen because the records already pointed at it -- the `price_type` instruction names it while the select itself said nothing; **TEN** call sites in `app/` read `price_type == 'inclusive' && shop_price == 'including'` or its negation, so either setting alone changes nothing. (b) the `role` select on `users/create` and `users/edit`, one shared sentence, three claims each read off the write path. (c) `order/view`'s two toolbar selects, described **ONCE** -- and that shape is MEASURED, not preferred: each column is `d-flex` row at **188x40 at 1440px and 340x40 at 390px**, so a per-control hint either sits beside the select and halves it or, stacked, wraps to 7-8 lines and takes the toolbar from 40px to ~190px. The fact is shared too -- both POST on `change` with no Save button and no confirmation -- so one sentence in its own full-width column describes both exactly, and `aria-describedby` may name one id from more than one control. The symptom is as described; the fix is a loop in that one handler, not new machinery. Worth knowing because that handler's own docblock records this class of omission happening THREE times already (the campaign breakdown shipping to the screen and not the file, `retention` repeating it, and the map-versus-source drift `FigureExportTest` now guards); and RULE 5 NEEDS NOTHING -- `routes/web.php` already attaches `PlatformOperatorOnly` to both `platform.analytics` and `platform.analytics.export`, both figures render inside those handlers, and no route is added. So: build the four, register the three questions, leave `redemptions` on its gate. **SHIPPED 2026-11-24, ALL FOUR THAT WERE BUILDABLE. `devices` (`3e9f9339`), `profit`, `visits` and `sources`: so of the eight, FOUR remain and EVERY ONE OF THEM IS BLOCKED -- `conversion` on `D-N32`, `channels` on `D-N41`, `campaigns` on `D-N42`, `redemptions` on its data gate. There is no remaining counterpart engineering can build without an owner decision, which is the state this row should be read in. `profit` reuses `StoreRevenueMargin(true)` -- the same metric and the same settled-only flag `/finance` and `/analytics` pass -- so the DEFINITION is not re-decided, only the scope widens, and `total()` is never called: one row per currency, no sum, because no historical exchange rate exists in this schema. ONE DELIBERATE DIFFERENCE FROM THE MERCHANT TWIN, recorded because it looks like an omission: the merchant tiles run `byCurrency()` through `MoneyText::buckets(..., true)`, which drops a bucket rounding to zero; this does NOT, because across tenants a currency whose margin nets to approximately zero is the statement "that group broke even" and dropping the row would make the screen silently narrower than the data. `payments()` iterates raw for the same reason and this follows it. The cost caveat (`rows_without_cost`) is a SECOND and different coverage question from `LedgerCoverage`'s and is carried separately, as a count of orders naming no money. **TWO OF MY OWN TEST ASSERTIONS WERE SATISFIED BY ANOTHER SECTION OF THE SAME PAGE** -- a deleted "Not available" line and an English-hard-coded `Currency` header both survived whole-page assertions, because the empty `devices` breakdown and the `payments` table carry those same strings; every markup assertion is now scoped to its own section through a helper, which is this file's own standing caution about unscoped censuses arriving at the markup layer. `visits` is a page-view COUNT and is NOT blocked by `D-N32`, because a count is not the contested ratio; it carries two coverage statements neither money figure needs -- the LATEST attribution start among the stores that have logged a view (visit rows carried no `store_id` until 2026-11-20 and nothing backfills them) and the count of stores that have never logged one, whose zero cannot be told apart from "never recorded". Two aggregate statements, no visit row loaded into PHP, asserted on the SQL. **THE INDEPENDENT FINANCIAL REVIEW REFUTED THIS SLICE'S OWN CENTRAL CLAIM.** "CURRENCIES ARE NEVER ADDED" was false: `MetricResult::make()` re-keys every bucket through `CurrencySymbol::code()`, so EVERY unparseable value collapses into the single `UNKNOWN_CURRENCY` key and is summed there -- and because `store_order_financials.currency_code` is written from the per-store setting that `FIN-SYM` measured to hold SYMBOLS, that is ordinary data. Driven by the reviewer: `+1000.00` under `$` and `-2000.00` under a Syrian-pound symbol rendered as ONE row reading `-1,000.00`, beneath a caption saying the rows are not added together. For payments the row is merely inflated; for a MARGIN the sum can REVERSE THE SIGN. The fold itself is NOT changed -- it lives in `MetricResult`, every money surface shares it, and splitting the bucket by raw stored value would change what `/finance` and `/analytics` report, which is a shipped financial definition and the owner's. Both tables now disclose the row. **THREE MORE REVIEW FINDINGS CHANGED CODE:** the merchant tile's TAX clause had been dropped, which `verify-metric-layer-on-mysql.php` already asserts for the merchant half under the name "and says what it leaves out TRUTHFULLY" -- and it matters MORE across tenants, because `TaxOption` is per store so one currency row can combine tax-inclusive and tax-exclusive margins; `FigureCsv::currencyRows()` plained only the AMOUNT, so bidi isolates reached the download's disclosure column, falsifying that class's own claim that the invariant is total (`rows()` and `breakdownRows()` were fixed on 2026-11-22 and the third builder was missed -- this leaked for `payments` too); and skipping `MoneyText::buckets()` to keep zero rows also skipped its `ksort()`, so the currency rows had no guaranteed order on MySQL, which SQLite cannot show. **AND MY OWN MUTATION RUN FOUND A HOLE IN THE EXPORT GUARD:** it caught a figure added to the screen and not to the file, but NOT the reverse -- deleting a figure from `forPlatform()` left its label-map entry, `FigureCsv::rows()` skipped it silently, and the expectation, derived from the now-shorter figure array, expected nothing. The map is now read from `PlatformAnalyticsController::figureLabels()` and asserted in both directions. **FOUR OF MY OWN ASSERTIONS WERE SATISFIED BY ANOTHER PART OF THE SAME PAGE** -- a deleted "Not available" line, an English-hard-coded `Currency` header, and two `visits` tests that passed before `visits()` existed at all (its words are in the `devices` hint and its query in `devices()`'s own aggregate). Markup assertions are now scoped to their section, tiles are read as label/value PAIRS, and the SQL premise names visits' own two statements. **RECORDED, NOT FIXED:** `product_cost <= 0` cannot tell "cost not recorded" from a genuine zero-cost line (no better signal exists in the schema, and the merchant twin says the same); `payment_status = 'paid'` drops partially-paid rows in full with no count of them, which the cross-tenant COD population makes larger; one tenant's single dated order outside the ledger narrows the window for every money figure on the screen (pre-existing in `payments` and `retention`, no request found that causes it); and the PHP-side `__()` strings in the metric layer are covered by NO translation census -- `ArabicUiCoverageTest` scans Blade files only -- so the merchant profit hint is English-only to this day. The one such sentence this figure prints is now translated. **`sources` IS NOT A VISIT BREAKDOWN**, which is why it needs no row cap while `channels` and `campaigns` do: `orders.order_source` is written by the WRITER that created the order from a closed three-value vocabulary and is never inferred, so there is nothing to decide, whereas their buckets are VISITOR-AUTHORED and what a cap MEANS across tenants is exactly what `D-N41`/`D-N42` hold. It folds NULL and the empty string into one absence (two rows both labelled as an absence let a reader take one for two channels), ranks in PHP with ONE orderer because the fold changes the order after any SQL `ORDER BY`, shows an unnamed stored value rather than dropping it from a count whose label says every order, and carries the ORDERS-not-visits sentence, which is more load-bearing here than on the merchant screen because this table renders directly beneath a cross-tenant page-view COUNT. **TWO OF ITS MUTATIONS SURVIVED FIRST AND BOTH WERE ACCIDENTS OF THE TEST ENGINE:** deleting `arsort()` passed, because SQLite returns `GROUP BY` groups with NULL and the empty string FIRST, so the folded bucket was already rendering first whether or not anything sorted it -- a second case with the sizes reversed is the one a mutation can see; and deleting the zero-store branch passed, because this figure does NOT go through `MetricScope` -- `whereIn('store_id', [])` compiles to `0 = 1` rather than throwing, so the empty-result sentence took over and said "No orders were placed in this period", which is a different and FALSE claim when there is no store at all. That guard stands between the reader and a false statement rather than between the screen and an exception, and the test now names the statement. Profit 11 tests, visits 11, sources 11; 50 of 50 mutations caught across seven rounds (5 survived first and all five were closed), two independent reviews (financial and tenancy). **NOT A WIDENED AUDIENCE:** the tenancy review read both doors and confirmed `PlatformOperatorOnly` on the screen and the export, no request input anywhere in the new path, and an explicit `store_id` predicate on every query. It does note, as a product fact rather than a defect, that cost and MARGIN are a new CATEGORY of merchant-confidential data on this screen, and that a currency traded by only one store makes that row that merchant's exact gross margin -- which the requirement's own words ("the same metrics across tenants", and estimated gross profit is one of the merchant screen's twelve) authorise, and which is recorded here rather than decided again. Then lead (a), which removes the cause rather than the symptom, and (g), which is the same screen's remaining unlabelled figures. `D-N34` needs a read-only count from production before anything else about it can be said. The F257 question is on the owner's register as `D-N31`. |

the shopper's ticket list and both order pickers in `AccountProfileController` had no store predicate from any source; `ticket_id = time()` gave two shoppers in one second one number; `SupportTicket::$fillable` declared a column that does not exist). **The test that claimed to cover the reply door posted `description` where the handler validates `reply_description`, so it passed on the open door** — corrected, with a control. `SUPPORT-004` temporary, scoped support access with merchant consent and an audit log — **PARTIAL**: the AUDIT half exists and is operator-only (`app/Support/Audit/RecordImpersonation.php`, `app/Policies/UserPolicy.php:385-401`, `app/Http/Controllers/StoreController.php:1085-1110`); temporary, scoped and merchant-consent are all absent — an impersonation session has no expiry, no reduced permission set and no consent record. `SUPPORT-005` separate emergency access with higher permissions and approvals — **ABSENT**: no second path, no approval mechanism, and `UserPolicy::impersonate` is the only grant. | `SUPPORT-003` is closed and needs nothing further. The other four each need a decision this container cannot take, so none is deferred — all are OPEN. (a) `SUPPORT-001`'s teams and SLA, `SUPPORT-002`'s announcements, `SUPPORT-004`'s consent and time-box record and `SUPPORT-005`'s approval trail each need a NEW TABLE, which CLAUDE.md §2 makes an owner escalation (persistent migration); nothing was migrated here. (b) `support_tickets.ticket_id` still has NO unique index — the generator now carries entropy, which makes a collision improbable rather than impossible, and the index is the structural fix and the same escalation. (c) `SUPPORT-001`'s knowledge base and videos overlap Phase 03's separately scoped bilingual Documentation Center; ask the owner which phase owns them before building either. (d) `SUPPORT-004`/`SUPPORT-005` need the duration, the permission subset and the approver named by the owner, then the `tenant-authz-reviewer` on the implementation. adding it to the harness and to the ratchet's view list made SLICE 1's OWN TEST FAIL and name both controls, and took the baseline from 74 to 94. **So slice 1's surface count above reads 24 and should read 25**; its '30 conversions, 7 measured' figures stand. **AND THE OBVIOUS REPAIR WAS INERT FOR TWO OF THREE, which is the finding worth carrying forward:** putting the tone class on the failing element changed nothing, and the re-measurement said so in the clearest way by returning the same ratios under the NEW selectors -- `.plan-card-detail li` is (0,1,1) and outranks a single class, and `.plan_card .card.price-card .card-body span.text-sm` is (0,4,1) AND carries `!important`. The remedy needed no new CSS and no `!important`: wrap the text in a `<span>` the host selector cannot match, which is what the design system already prescribes for the heading case. The Yes/No rows wore three host badge classes answering one question, so the set moved together while keeping the neutral/warning distinction; `bg-warning` is removed rather than joined because it carries `!important`. | `GAP-117` | `P03-MP-01` | 03 Storefront, The | PARTIALLY IMPLEMENTED | **THE REQUIREMENT IS RECOVERABLE AND THIS ROW'S OWN SOURCE LINE WAS STALE.** The traceability section says *"the plan itself is not in the repo"* — it was recovered 2026-11-16, and the obligation is one Arabic bullet, the last of Phase 03 §`03-B` تجربة العميل, family `CUST-001`…`CUST-008`, at `master-plan/MASTER-PRODUCT-PLAN-v1.0-BASE.md:222`: *تحسين SEO والفهرسة والأداء والصور والبيانات المنظمة* — **FIVE** obligations (SEO, indexing, performance, images, structured data), not one. Verified by reading line 222. The id-to-bullet pairing is FAMILY-LEVEL ONLY (8 ids, 9 bullets) and was not guessed. **THREE OF THE FIVE ARE NOW BUILT (2026-11-26)**, against a measured baseline of 0 canonical, 0 robots, 0 JSON-LD and no sitemap across all four layouts: a route-derived `rel=canonical`; a fail-closed per-route `robots` directive with a CI-blocking census that reddens until a new storefront route is ruled on either way; `Organization` JSON-LD; and a per-store `{storeSlug}/sitemap.xml`. Emitted from `metaKeywordSetting()`, **the only call site in all FOUR layout copies** — the appearance partial is in three, so choosing it would have left a theme uncovered — and no `themes/` file was edited. **THE CENSUS FOUND WHAT READING THE ROUTE FILE DID NOT:** `theme.draft.preview` renders the storefront layout for an UNPUBLISHED theme version and its URI does not BEGIN with the slug, so a first-segment derivation missed it; indexing it would have published a store's unreleased design. **TWO MEASUREMENTS CORRECT EXISTING BELIEFS:** a deactivated store's storefront renders 200 in production (`ActiveTheme`'s `abort('403')` is commented out, and `Theme::set()` prepends a view root that survives the whole PROCESS, so under php-fpm the 200 path is ordinary), which makes the `noindex` load-bearing; and `route()` does NOT percent-encode `&` in a path segment, so one ampersand in a product slug makes the WHOLE sitemap malformed, which makes the `ENT_XML1` escaping load-bearing rather than defensive. 69 tests / 285 assertions, re-run here rather than taken from the report; 43 of 46, 10 of 11 and 4 of 5 failed against the unwired code; **36 mutations, 36 caught — one survived the first run as a real coverage gap**, `Store::find(getCurrentStore()) ?? …` serving MERCHANT A's catalogue at store B's sitemap URL, with no case holding a `web`-guard principal to notice. A hand-run authz pass found two things in the change itself, both fixed: an unthrottled anonymous route running up to 50,000-row queries, and the sitemap listing `'auction'`/`'digital'` products that every storefront listing excludes — which would have CHANGED their discoverability rather than described it. | **TWO OWNER DECISIONS, both refusals with measured reasons rather than omissions.** (1) hreflang ar/en: the storefront's language switch is `Cookie::queue('LANGUAGE', …)` with NO per-language URL, so every alternate would point at the page itself — a URL-level language dimension is a public-URL-shape decision across ~75 routes plus a redirect policy for every existing link, and a test pins the absence so nobody adds a decorative one. (2) `public/robots.txt` is untouched: host-root, shared by every tenant AND the platform's own site, so its contents and the `Sitemap:` line are a platform-operator call. Engineering remainder: `Product` structured data deliberately held back until the displayed price has one source — so "product/organization structured data" is HALF delivered; no sitemap cache or index past the enforced 50,000 cap; `page.custom` and `page.article` live in module tables that load in no test run; module storefront routes have no verdict and so get `noindex`; no merchant-facing surface; and a MySQL-only case-folding duplicate-URL case SQLite's BINARY collation cannot see. **`routes/web.php` still needs one line** — `StorefrontSitemapRoute::declare();` after the `customer.php` require. The images obligation of the same plan bullet is now `GAP-274` |
| `GAP-118` | `P03-MP-02` | 03 Storefront, The | VERIFIED COMPLETE | A merchant-configurable feature is wired to nothing. Also worth flagging while here: metaKeywordSetting interpolates its arguments into single-quoted HTML attributes with no escaping, so it would be an injection sink the | **DONE 2026-11-25, both halves of the next action, and the baseline was measured before anything changed.** MEASURED at HEAD on four stores that each had all three values stored, across all twelve storefront pages that answer 200: **ten of the twelve `<meta>` tags carried `config('app.name')`** — the PLATFORM's name — on every page of every store, `og:image` included, where a URL belongs. Only `og:url` and `twitter:url` were per-store, because `$slug` was the one argument the layout passed. The merchant half was always live (`POST /seo-setting`, route `seo.settings`, stores all three names under the merchant's own `store_id`); nothing read them back. **THE READ-BACK WENT IN THE VIEW COMPOSER, AND NO BLADE FILE WAS EDITED.** `App\Providers\ViewServiceProvider`'s composer is registered for the view NAME `front_end.layouts.app`, under which **all four copies** of that layout resolve, and each already called `metaKeywordSetting($metakeyword ?? null, ...)` — so one registration feeds every copy, which is the outcome to want given that a layout-level change made in fewer than four places silently does nothing for the other themes. MEASURED which copy serves which store (`View::creator` reporting `$view->getPath()`): `stylique`/`greentic`/`techzonix` each get their own, `theme_id = ''` gets stylique's via `config('theme.active')`, and an uninstalled theme name answers **503 before the composer runs** — so the `resources/views` fallback copy is reached by NO store and no HTTP test can cover it; `tests/Unit/Ui/StorefrontSeoMetaCallSitesTest` is its only cover and closes the four-copy census in both directions. **THE TENANCY TRAP WAS THE WHOLE RISK AND IS WHY `$store->id` IS PASSED EXPLICITLY.** `Utility::GetValueByName($name)` with no store id defaults to `getCurrentStore() ?: getRouteStoreId()`, and `getCurrentStore()` reads `current_store` off the DEFAULT (`web`) guard; storefront routes sit in the `web` group with a session cookie path of `/`, so a merchant with a live panel session is a populated web-guard principal on every storefront request — the default would have printed store A's SEO metadata into store B's `<head>` for that visitor. The composer passes the store it already resolved from `request()->route('storeSlug')`. Two tests drive exactly that principal and are the only ones that redden when the store id is dropped. **THE ESCAPING IS NOW LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN LATENT, which is the one thing this closure changes about the row's own second sentence.** Before the wiring all three arguments were always null, so the raw interpolation was unreachable by any request; `Utility::GetValueByName` applies no filter to these names (its only transform is the currency-symbol cleaner, which ignores them), so a stored value now arrives verbatim at a `{!! !!}` print and `e()` is the whole of the defence. Pinned at the helper (`tests/Unit/Security/MetaKeywordSettingEscapingTest`, 22 tests) **and at the rendered page** (4 payloads driven end to end through a real storefront request). No separate row: this row's own text names the sink and its next action names the fix. **EVIDENCE.** `tests/Feature/Storefront/StorefrontSeoMetaPerStoreTest` — 31 tests, 190 assertions; **27 of the 31 failed against the unwired code**, measured by reverting the three shared keys and re-running rather than inferred, and the 4 that passed were the two premises and the two fallback guards, which is the correct shape. Plus `StorefrontSeoMetaCallSitesTest` (6) and the two Job 1 files (22 + 4). **36 mutations, all caught**, including one per copy of each of the eight value prints (a value printed three times is three mutants), `ENT_COMPAT`, a missing `ENT_SUBSTITUTE`, double encoding, the wrong argument escaped, dropping the store id, substituting `getCurrentStore()`, a fixed store id, and a comment-trap mutant that names the variable only inside a `{{-- --}}`. Bilingual AR/RTL and EN/LTR both driven on one Arabic-valued store. **Three things deliberately NOT done, each a decision rather than an omission:** the vendor's `metakeyword` -> `name='title'`/`og:title`/`twitter:title` mapping is preserved although the merchant field is labelled *Meta Keywords* (there is no title setting to read, and inventing one is product scope); **no `<meta name='keywords'>` tag was added**, so the one stored value a crawler would read as keywords still reaches only the title slots; and the pre-existing leading space inside five `content='` values is untouched. **ONE DEFECT IN THE SAME STATEMENT WAS WIDER THAN THIS ROW'S WORDING AND WAS THE ONLY LIVE ONE OF THE THREE — recorded here rather than given its own id, which the integrator may still prefer to do.** The helper ended in `return sprintf($script);` — the fully interpolated markup passed as the FORMAT argument with nothing after it, so every `%` in it was read as a conversion specification. `$url` is `route('landing_page', $slug)`, which percent-encodes, so an Arabic store slug (`متجر-سوريا` -> `%D9%85%D8%AA…`) raised `ArgumentCountError: 37 arguments are required, 1 given`. The helper is called at line 12 of the layout, inside `<head>`, so that was **HTTP 500 on every storefront page of that store** — needing no merchant SEO value at all, because `$slug` was always supplied, and on a product whose first-class languages are Arabic and English an Arabic store name is the ordinary case. Unlike the injection this was reachable before any wiring. `sprintf()` returns its format unchanged whenever it does not throw, so returning the string directly is behaviour-preserving for every input that already worked, and an ASCII-slug control proves it. Pinned twice — at the helper and by a real storefront request against an Arabic-slug store (`tests/Feature/Storefront/ArabicSlugStorefrontRendersTest`) — and both pins verified by restoring `sprintf($script)` and watching each fail. |

| `GAP-129` | `P04-P1-03` | 04 Commerce (P4-0  | PARTIALLY IMPLEMENTED | **ALL THREE NAMED DEFECTS NOW HAVE A VERDICT, AND FOR ONE OF THEM THE WORD 'UNTOUCHED' WAS ALREADY STALE WHEN THIS ROW WAS WRITTEN.** **P4-F004 (guest basket identity) DID NOT REPRODUCE:** it was fixed on 2026-11-22 by `App\Support\Commerce\GuestBasketIdentity::forRequest()` (the writer takes the basket from the `cart` cookie and falls back to the session only when there is none), and the reproduction passes at HEAD. Re-measured rather than taken on trust: reverting the resolver, and separately the writer, reddens all three of `GuestBasketIdentityTest`'s assertions, so the containment is live. **P4-F005 (removal no-op) REPRODUCED AND IS FIXED:** `POST {slug}/change-cart` with `quantity_type=remove` answered HTTP 200 and `{"count":1,"status":1,...Cart successfully updated.}` with the row and its quantity 2 untouched, because `unset($cart_array->$cart_id)` removed a dynamic property from the basket ROW and the trailing `save()` is gated on `isDirty()`. It now deletes the row, inside an `else` so that `save()` cannot re-INSERT a deleted model. **P4-F011 (login-merge duplication) REPRODUCED AND IS FIXED:** a customer holding 2 of a product who signed in carrying a guest basket holding 1 ended with TWO lines for it (ids 1 and 2, quantities 2 and 1, both cookies null), and a `+` press on the SECOND line then moved the FIRST from 2 to 3 and left the pressed one at 1. The merge now folds the guest quantity into the line the account already holds and deletes the guest row, keyed on `Cart::forCustomer(auth('customers')->id())` plus the route's store. 10 new tests in 2 new files (`tests/Feature/Commerce/GuestCartRemoveTest.php` and `LoginCartMergeTest.php`, 55 assertions), beside the 10 that already pin P4-F004; 16 mutations applied one at a time, all 16 caught. **WHAT KEEPS THE ROW OPEN, AND NEITHER PART IS FIRST-PARTY CODE THIS CHANGE MAY TOUCH:** the two vendored twins of P4-F004 still mint a session id per add and overwrite the cookie, so with `BoostSales` or `FrequentlyProduct` active one add through their routes renames the basket again. The P4-F004 disposition records a host-side guard as an owner decision NOT taken, and CLAUDE.md 4.1 forbids reading an engineering deferral as an owner decision, so this cannot be counted as deferred. And `ApiController::cart_qty` still re-identifies the pressed line by product-plus-variant tuple with no `orderBy`; the duplicate it misreads now has no producer left, which removes the input rather than fixing the reader, and that file is another workstream's. | Put the two vendored twins to the owner as a decision: a host-side guard at `App\Http\Middleware\ModuleCartWriteGuard`, which already intercepts both module actions, or an accepted exception recorded against P4-F004. (b) F061's two UI halves: the storefront module page `GET /{storeSlug}/coupons` lists coupons with no status filter, so an inactive coupon stays publicly advertised (`packages/workdo/CouponList`, vendored and not editable), and the merchant's own coupon list renders no status column, so the toggle's state is invisible outside the edit modal. **THE LIVE-COUPON QUESTION, ANSWERED BY MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN DECIDED, AND IT STILL HAS ONE OWNER-VISIBLE EDGE.** Nothing persists a coupon acceptance between requests on the storefront: both cart arithmetics re-resolve the code on every read, so the gate cannot be scoped to "new applications" even in principle -- an UNSUBMITTED basket already showing the discount loses it at the next cart read or at checkout, and that is forced by the architecture, not chosen. | `GAP-143` | `P04-P8-02` | 04 Commerce (P4-0  | PARTIALLY IMPLEMENTED | **THE ACCEPTANCE CLAUSE IS NOW EXERCISED ON A STORED ORDER (2026-11-26), AND THE DERIVATION IT EXERCISES OVERCHARGES.** The fixture is seeded and driven end to end -- a zone on the shopper's country and state, a method in that zone at 25.00, a 10% band naming the jurisdiction, the shopper's own address, 2 x 100.00 of goods -- through `get-shipping-data` (zone resolution and method offering), `shipping-method` (cost and tax), `get-tax-data` (band derivation) and `process-order` with `payment_type=cod`, then reconciled against the **`orders` row the shipped checkout wrote** using the writer's own component map: `product_price` 220.00, `tax_price` 22.00, `delivery_price` 25.00, `final_price` 267.00, and `OrderTotalReconciler::reconcile()` answers `matches: true`. **The CURRENCY question is answered by measurement as a CODE DEFECT, not a fixture artefact, and fixed in two lines.** Both storefront endpoints that emit a `CURRENCY` key filled it from `defult_currancy_symbol`, a name `Utility::Setting()`'s `$defaults` does not carry and `defaultSetting()` does not seed; of the four store-creation paths **only `StoreController` seeds it**, so **every store created by merchant self-registration or by the admin app served `CURRENCY: null` from both endpoints for its whole life**, while the formatted figures in the same response read `$` from `currency_format_with_sym()`'s own hardcoded fallback -- one response, two answers about one store's currency. Now read through `OrderCurrencyStamp::symbolFor()`, the single place P5-R14 made responsible for a store's symbol. **Verified by the integrator rather than taken on report:** the swap keeps the same primary setting name and only adds a fallback, so it cannot return a different value where a row exists; `defult_currancy_symbol` is in `CurrencySymbol::SETTING_NAMES`, so the P1-SEC-80 sanitiser still applies; no new query, no request input, and both files' line counts are unchanged at 1797 so the four `file:line` inventories needed no re-pointing. Two alternatives were rejected by measurement: teaching `$defaults` the name would change **the symbol stamped on new orders**, which is recorded financial data; reading `CURRENCY` instead would ignore a merchant who changed their symbol through the admin app, which writes only the other name. Blast radius stated honestly: both JS consumers of this key are commented out, so this was a latent contract defect rather than a `null` on a shopper's screen -- fixed because the next consumer to be uncommented would print it beside a price. Evidence: 15 tests / 110 assertions, **13 mutations all caught**, and three mutants that survived their first round were closed by finding the input that makes the behaviours disagree -- never by weakening the mutation -- which added two genuine cross-tenant assertions, including a neighbour tenant holding a zone in the SAME jurisdiction seeded first, because the lookup is a `->first()` with no `orderBy`. | **NOT ADVANCED TO VERIFIED COMPLETE, and that is the integrator's call against the implementing agent's own recommendation.** The agent argued VERIFIED COMPLETE and offered this alternative honestly; the reason to take the alternative is its own finding (a). **(a) THE MERCHANT'S TAX BAND IS APPLIED TWICE TO WHAT THE SHOPPER IS CHARGED, MEASURED THROUGH THE SHIPPED COD CHECKOUT.** `Product::ProductPrice()` adds the 10% band to reach 220.00, and the same band is then applied again to that 220.00 as `tax_price` 22.00, so the charge is **267.00 where a single application on 200.00 of goods plus 25.00 of carriage is 245.00** -- 22.00 of tax the merchant never configured, collected by the COD driver. A requirement about trustworthy shipping and tax derivation cannot be called verified while the derivation it exercises overcharges, even though the components reconcile. **No existing pin could see it, because every one of them is an "adds up" check:** the work queue records this arithmetic as S21.4 on the API's `place_order`, where `tax_price` is written 0 and the components do NOT add up, which is the shape the existing known-bad test pins; the web writer is the opposite shape, writing the real 22.00 and building `final_price` from the subtotal, so they DO add up and the double count is CHARGED. Now pinned by a test that fails with the corrected total if anyone fixes it. **Not fixed here: it changes what every taxed order is charged, which CLAUDE.md §2 reserves to the owner.** (b) A new cross-tenant finding is recorded as **`GAP-278`**. Residuals that belong to other rows, named so they are not read as this one's: the ~20 gateway-return writers and the two API writers are not reconciled with shipping and tax present (`GAP-132` / `P04-P8-03`); the arithmetic is on SQLite decimals only (`P04-P8-01` already records it); and `GAP-262` stays open. |

With no symbol row of its own, a store's `get-tax-data` reports the **neighbour merchant's** symbol to a viewer holding a panel session for that neighbour. The mechanism is the fall-through `GAP-230` already records: `Utility::Setting($storeId)` **ignores its store-id argument whenever `Auth::check()` is true** and reads `getCurrentStore()` instead, and storefront routes sit in the `web` group with a session cookie path of `/`, so a merchant with a live panel login is a populated web-guard principal on every storefront request -- the dual-session hazard CLAUDE.md §1 warns about, on a money label. **Measured BOTH ways**, under the GAP-143 fix and under the original line restored and re-run, so that change neither causes nor cures it. Severity is a **mislabelled price for one viewer** rather than a disclosure: a store's symbol is printed on every price of its public storefront, so the wrong one is wrong for everyone who sees it, but nothing of the neighbour's leaks beyond the symbol itself. Pinned at the measured behaviour by a known-bad test -- **invert it when fixed, do not relax it.** | **Named remedy, mirroring a pattern already in the repository twice:** a `configuredSymbolFor(int $storeId): ?string` on `OrderCurrencyStamp` built exactly like its existing `configuredCodeFor()` -- a raw query with an explicit `store_id` predicate, `orderByDesc('id')` because `settings` has no unique index, and `CurrencySymbol::cleanSetting()` -- read at the two `CartController` sites with a default fallback, leaving `symbolFor()` byte-for-byte unchanged so the ORDER writers' stamped symbol does not move. `DocumentDirection::ownRow()` is the other precedent. This wants a `tenant-authz-reviewer` pass when it is fixed, because it is a tenancy predicate on a path a dual-session principal reaches. |

## Category R — recovered on 2026-11-16, never previously tracked (18)

These are **not new work items the project invented**. They are owner-approved requirements whose
text was unavailable until the Master Product Plan was recovered, so the 350-row matrix never
carried them in any status — not complete, not partial, not deferred. They are listed here to
satisfy the standing rule that no original requirement may be silently lost.

**Status for all eighteen is `COVERAGE NOT ASSESSED`, deliberately.** `ARCH-009` — itself recovered
today — says: *do not infer a feature's absence in production from the absence of its evidence in
the repository.* Each row below therefore records what was **measured in this container** and stops
there. None is marked NOT IMPLEMENTED, and none is marked complete.

**No category A/B/C/D/E is assigned.** Six of the eighteen fall in Phases 03 and 04, which are
already declared engineering-complete; that is a disposition only the owner can make.

**AND THIS BLOCK IS NOT THE WHOLE CENSUS.** Two more were found on 2026-11-24 and are recorded
below as `GAP-231`/`GAP-232`; the never-carried total is **twenty**, not eighteen.

| `GAP-192` | `SAAS-007` | 06 | **Half-tracked.** `GMV` appears 4× in the matrix; **`MRR` and `ARR` appear zero times.** Separating platform revenue from merchant sales is the half that was never stated. **BUILT 2026-11-25, AND NOT CLOSED.** `App\Services\SaasRevenueFigures` now computes MRR, ARR and paying-merchant counts from `plans`/`users`, and plan receipts per currency from `plan_orders`, on two operator-only screens (`/superadmin` revenue and resources); 91 tests. **Every figure states its own period, basis and exclusions**, the receipts rows are never added across currencies (the schema records no rate per payment date), and the screen says in its own words that this is NOT what shoppers pay merchants. **An independent financial review raised fifteen findings and three were real corrections to the producer:** the receipt bucket key and the platform denomination both used `strtoupper(trim())` where the project's single definition of "is a currency code" is `CurrencySymbol::code()`, so a `price_currency` of `Dollar` got its own row and split one real currency in two; `MoneyText::buckets()` was called with its default `dropEmpty`, so a SETTLED payment of 0.00 — an ordinary free-plan or 100%-coupon purchase — vanished from the screen entirely, row AND order count; and ARR was derived from the ROUNDED MRR, losing 0.04 on a single yearly plan priced at 100. All three are fixed and pinned, four mutations, all caught. **A fourth finding was refuted by measurement and the refutation is the useful part:** `plan_orders.price` is `$table->float('price')`, which is GAP-217's float-precision basis, so `MetricCaveat::FLOAT_PRECISION` was attached to both receipt figures — then `verify-saas-revenue-on-mysql.php` showed Laravel 11's `float()` emits a MySQL **`double`** and that `2500000.49` at SYP scale round-trips exactly, so the caveat came back out and its ABSENCE is now pinned. **AN IMPLICATION FOR OTHER SURFACES THAT THIS SLICE DID NOT MEASURE AND MUST NOT ASSERT:** `MetricCaveat::FLOAT_PRECISION` is applied elsewhere on the stated basis that the figure comes off a `float` column (`orders.final_price`). If those columns are also declared with Laravel's `float()`, the same `double` mapping may apply and the caveat may be overstating a limit on screens that carry it. **This was NOT measured here** — `orders.final_price`'s real DDL was never read in this slice — and the caveat is a conservative claim, so nothing was changed. It is named so the next reader of those surfaces measures the column instead of inheriting the wording. | **The requirement is not closed and the remaining work is named.** (1) **The 12x overstatement is CLOSED on the read side, 2026-11-25, and the write side is still open.** `plans.duration` is `string nullable` free text, `Plan::$arrDuration` defines exactly `Unlimited|Month|Year`, and `PlanController`'s store and update rules cover `name`, `price`, `max_stores`, `max_products`, `max_users` and `storage_limit` and **do not mention `duration`** — the form is a `Form::select`, which constrains the UI and not the request. The producer branched `$duration === 'Year' ? $price / 12 : $price`, so every other spelling went down the monthly branch at its WHOLE price: a plan priced 1200 a year reading `Yearly` reported MRR 1200 instead of 100. An unrecognised duration now goes to a named, visible `Duration not recognised` bucket with its reason, rather than being counted or dropped — the same refusal `Unlimited` and `plan_missing` already get, and NOT a synonym table, which would be this class inventing the figure it exists to stop inventing. Two tests (the `Yearly` case and a premise that `Year` still reaches MRR), mutation caught. **Still open:** no server-side rule constrains what `PlanController` accepts, so bad durations can still be WRITTEN; that is a validation change on a plan-creation contract and belongs with the owner's duration-vocabulary decision. (2) The screens are operator-only by route; no merchant-facing or exportable form of these figures exists. (3) GAP-253 records that the slice's two `selectRaw` statements are counted by no raw-query inventory. (4) The owner has not accepted either screen. Phase 06 remains the owning phase. |

## The NINETEENTH and TWENTIETH untracked requirements — `ADM-001`, `ADM-002` (found 2026-11-24)

**The census above was itself incomplete, and this is the finding.** `DECISION-ID-REGISTER.md` §4.3
measured which recovered requirements the 350-row matrix never carried, found fifteen plus three
half-tracked, and those eighteen became `GAP-182`…`GAP-199`. **`ADM-001` and `ADM-002` are not among
them and are not in the matrix or this register either** — so Store 360° and the platform command
dashboard were missed by the matrix, missed by this register, and missed by the audit whose entire
purpose was finding what the matrix had missed.

**How it stayed hidden, which is the part worth keeping.** `P06-02`…`P06-06` were derived from
CLAUDE.md §4 and `PROJECT-STATE.md`'s one-line Phase 06 summaries, and neither summary contains the
word *dashboard* or *360*. A requirement absent from the summary a derived row is written from
cannot appear in that row. Found by an adversarial verifier re-measuring a Phase 06 inventory brief.

**Measured, by grep, in this container:** `ADM-00` returns 5 hits in 3 files, every one of them
under `docs/development/master-plan/` — the recovered plan itself. **Zero** in
`MASTER-PLAN-REQUIREMENT-TRACEABILITY.md`, **zero** here. Seven vocabulary variants (*Store 360*,
*360°*, *store-360*, *command dashboard*, *platform command*, *platform dashboard*, and the Arabic
*مساحة*) each return 0/0 against both documents, so this is not a vocabulary miss by the searcher.

**Status for both is `COVERAGE NOT ASSESSED`,** for the same `ARCH-009` reason as the block above:
absence of evidence in this repository is not absence in production.

| gap | decision id(s) | phase | measured in this container | next action |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GAP-231` | `ADM-001` — *لوحة قيادة شاملة للمنصة* / a comprehensive platform command dashboard | 06 | **Partly present, and the word doing the work is *comprehensive*.** An operator dashboard exists (`HomeController`) and `/platform/analytics` exists with eight cross-tenant figures, nine routes carry `PlatformOperatorOnly`. Whether that constitutes the plan's *comprehensive command* dashboard is a product judgement, not a measurement. What IS measured and recorded against the existing one: *Popular Plan* reads every `plan_orders` row into PHP, three cards carry no `payment_status` predicate, *Total Store* counts MERCHANTS not stores, and one request path holds two definitions of the operator. | Owner: does `ADM-001` describe the dashboard that exists, a superset of it, or a replacement? Engineering cannot tell from the recovered one-line text. |
| `GAP-232` | `ADM-002` — *مساحة Store 360° لمراجعة حالة كل متجر وإدارته من أنظمته الأصلية* / a Store 360° space to review and manage each store from its own systems | 06 | **Not located.** `Store 360`, `store-360` and `360°` return **zero** hits across `app/`, `resources/`, `routes/` and `packages/workdo/`. The only per-store operator route is `stores/{id}/plan` (`routes/web.php:1134`); `StoreController@show` — the natural home of a per-store 360 view — is routed by `Route::resource('stores', …)` and its body is an **empty stub** (`app/Http/Controllers/StoreController.php:436-439`, literally `//`). | Phase 06 scope, not started. Expected absence as WORK; NOT an expected absence as a tracked requirement, which is why this row exists. |

**By phase: 06 → 2.** Both are Phase 06, which has not started, so neither is late work — the
defect is that neither was a *tracked requirement*.

## Gaps found by the MKT/ANA measurement of 2026-11-17

**These are NOT part of the `GAP-182`…`GAP-199` block above and do not change its tally.** That
block holds requirements that were missing from the matrix entirely. `GAP-200`…`GAP-203` are findings
against decision ids the matrix now traces (`P05-MKT-ANA`), surfaced when the eighteen unmeasured
`MKT-*` and `ANA-*` ids were measured against the code. Full detail and citations:
`docs/development/phase-5/PHASE-05-MKT-ANA-MEASUREMENT-2026-11-17.md`.

`GAP-204`…`GAP-211` are findings of the **PR #140 deletion audit** (what that diff removed or
narrowed), not of the MKT/ANA measurement; full detail, evidence and the read-only queries for the
owner are in `docs/security/P05-PR140-DELETION-AUDIT-DIGEST.md`. They are recorded here so the owner
decisions they carry are not lost, and — like `GAP-200`…`GAP-203` — they sit outside the 199-row
tally above. `GAP-214` is a finding of the D6 wiring measurement (`docs/development/phase-5/PHASE-05-D6-WIRING-MEASUREMENT.md`)
and sits outside the tally for the same reason. `GAP-215` is a finding of the independent tenant-authz
review of **D8-3** (the opt-in surface and the unsubscribe page) and likewise sits outside it; the
review's other four findings were engineering defects and are fixed, so only the one needing an owner
decision is recorded here.

| gap | decision id(s) | phase | measured | next action |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GAP-200` | `MKT-008` | 05 | **Consent is not enforced for four message senders, and no unsubscribe path exists.** `customers` has **no consent or eligibility column at all** (all 25 columns of the create migration read). The one real consent mechanism, `lynomia_consent_states`, is enforced *upstream* outside this repository and nothing in `app/` or `packages/workdo/` reads it to gate a send. Sending anyway with no consent predicate: `CouponEmail` on registration, on first order and on an order-value threshold (`CreateCustomerLis.php:28`, `CreateOrderLis.php:32,51`), `ReviewReminder` (`SendOrderReviewMailListner.php:38`, which gates only on the merchant's template being active), the newsletter list, and the back-in-stock push. Confidence: certain. | **CENSUS COMPLETE 2026-11-17** (`PHASE-05-MKT-008-SENDER-CENSUS-2026-11-17.md`): **214 send sites**, 40 classified, 174 enumerated-not-analysed, 10 of 40 corrected by an adversarial pass which found the first pass **over-reported promotional sending** (`whatsapp_messages` has no body column, so several "MIXED" sends are transactional; one sender is dead code) and found **one interception claim outright false**. **ENFORCEMENT AT THE SENDING BOUNDARY IS ACHIEVABLE with no migration and no vendored edit** — `MessageSending` is MEASURED aborting email sends (including vendored Mailables) by booting the real bootstrap; WhatsApp, FCM and the template family each have a first-party chokepoint in `app/Models/Utility.php`. **DURABLE SUPPRESSION STORAGE IS NOT**: `lynomia_consent_states` is a good design template but is vendored, unverifiable in production, and holds **no email or phone column**, and the production shape of `customers`/`newsletters`/`users` has never been measured. **OWNER DECISION, and it is blocked on two standing restrictions at once.** Every sender named is in a **vendored** `packages/workdo/` module, and a consent column is a **persistent migration**. Not actionable without an explicit owner instruction on both. This is the one finding in the set with a real-world compliance dimension rather than a scope one. **D8 DECIDED (owner, 2026-11-17): first-party store, per-channel enforcement, no inference, no backfill, production migration and activation separately authorised. STATE 2026-11-18 — BUILT LOCALLY, UNWIRED, NOT COMPLETE:** an append-only evidence table, a rebuildable projection, hashed **reusable** unsubscribe tokens (an earlier version of this row said "single-use"; that was wrong), a ledger and a send gate (`off` by default, `report`, `enforce`) — **142 tests / 532 assertions**, **35 of 35** reverted fixes make a test fail, migrations applied **nowhere** (but they sit in `database/migrations/`, so the first `php artisan migrate` in any environment creates three empty tables), the gate called from **one place, the email seam, which is inert while enforcement is `off`** (it classifies from the mail event against a registry of 12 Mailable classes, 2 Notification classes and 19 template names with the evidence for each, leaves what the code does not prove **undecided and reported**, and cannot attribute the vendored coupon mail to a store), no unsubscribe route, SMS/WhatsApp/push unwired, 174 of 214 sender sites still unclassified in the repository. **Its independent review found a live authorisation hole outside the seam (P1-SEC-64, fixed): any verified user could rewrite the platform-wide email template bodies.** **INDEPENDENT REVIEW 2026-11-18** (four read-only lenses): **two real defects, reproduced on MariaDB 10.11 before any change and fixed** — a withdrawal could be lost in the row the gate reads (log `granted, granted, withdrawn`, projection `granted`) and first-ever writers deadlocked even for *different* contacts (3 of 200 lost with the retry removed, 0 of 200 after); plus append-only gaps, a census that matched write verbs, evidence that could stop a withdrawal, and a gate with no failure mode — all fixed and pinned. **NOT FIXABLE WITHOUT THE OWNER, and contained rather than solved: phone identity is not canonical** (a withdrawal under `0912345678` does not bind `963912345678`; two country-less numbers can share a key), so `enforce` is **read as `report` for SMS and WhatsApp** until a country-aware normaliser exists — that needs a store-level default country, a phone-number library and a re-key of stored rows. Also awaiting the owner: token lifetime and retention, database-level append-only (trigger or grants), a keyed hash, per-store activation. **GAP-200 stays OPEN until every promotional path is gated.** Design, review record and honest state: `docs/development/phase-5/PHASE-05-MKT-008-ENFORCEMENT-DESIGN.md` §10. |
| `GAP-201` | `ANA-007`, `ANA-006` | 05 | **The honesty mechanism cannot reach a sighted merchant.** `<x-ss.stat>` renders its `hint` inside `<span class="ss-visually-hidden">`, and `public/css/store-syria-ds.css:2238-2246` clips that to 1×1 with `clip: rect(0,0,0,0)` and **no `:hover`, `:focus` or `:focus-within` reveal anywhere** — verified by hand 2026-11-17. **Ten** `:hint=` usages sit on that component across **six** files, nine of them precisely the Phase 05 sentences written to stop a figure being misread and the tenth a navigation hint. *(First measured as nine across five; a forward regex over the component tag body was truncated by a Blade comment containing `>`. Separately, the 12-key `MetricCaveat` vocabulary has **zero consumers** outside `app/Support/Metrics/` and `tests/`. | **CLOSED by P5-11 on 2026-11-17.** The hint now renders as a visible `<p class="ss-stat__hint">` associated with the figure by `aria-describedby`. Measured in Chromium in all four language/theme combinations: **5.98:1 light, 7.18:1 dark**, composited against the real painted background. The `MetricCaveat` half of this row is NOT closed — the vocabulary still has no consumer on a report screen. |

So a hand-crafted POST redeems below both `checkout_points` and `checkout_price`. **Separately:** `RewardClubPointController:45` writes `club_point_enabled` as `$request->club_point_enabled ?? 'off'` and omits it from the validator (`:26-31` covers the six settings), so a store can save all six with the programme OFF. Neither partial tests it, the server does not test it, but the vendored listener's `:35` guards the entire debit block on it — so in that configuration **the discount is applied and no points are debited and no ledger row is written**. Recurring and unrecorded. | **PARTIAL — D6 STAGE 1 IMPLEMENTED 2026-11-18, and D6 IS STILL NOT CLOSED.** `RewardPointDiscount::forOrder()` is now wired at exactly two places, both in `PaymentController`: the quote (`processRewardPoints()`), which decides once per checkout from the signed-in customer's own balance and writes `'on'`/`'off'` back onto the request (a merge, not a removal — a query-string copy survives a removal), and the WhatsApp/Telegram writer, which a direct POST reaches without any quote. Both refuse with the flag removed so the vendored listener takes its accrual branch rather than spending the balance for nothing. A guest's flag is overwritten at the guest arm of `getCartData()`, and the COD/bank writer now reads the signed-in customer's balance. `RewardRedemptionCheckoutGateTest`: 34 tests, 139 assertions, **25 of 25 mutants killed**; the 'zero call sites' census was updated on purpose to pin the two. **An independent review of the first version found a real leak in it and it is fixed:** reading the principal's balance at the COD writer let a multipart *file* part named `customer_id` commit a discounted order and fail before the debit (executed: 50.00 on a 100.00 basket, balance 500, no ledger row, HTTP 500); the same trigger already existed on the WhatsApp writer. Both are now refused at the intake. `isRedeemable()` also requires the accrual arithmetic the vendored listener runs before it debits to be computable (a zero `order_point_price` is a `DivisionByZeroError` after the commit). **NOT DONE: the gateway-return handlers** (generic `getProductStatus`, AuthorizeNet, PayU, NMI, Khalti, Easebuzz, PaiementPro, CinetPay) — see `GAP-214` and `docs/development/phase-5/PHASE-05-D6-WIRING-MEASUREMENT.md`. CI's verdict on this change is the pull request's.  |
| `GAP-202` | `MKT-005`, `MKT-006` | 05 | **The vendored reward listener destroys a customer's point balance on orders that redeemed nothing, and its anti-tamper check has never executed.** `packages/workdo/RewardClubPoint/src/app/Listeners/AddRewardClubPointLis.php:38` mis-binds `??` against `==`/`&&`, collapsing to `$flag ?? false` — the truthiness of the posted `club_point_is_active`. **`'off'` is a non-empty string and therefore truthy, and `'off'` is what the shipped checkout posts for a customer who already qualifies to redeem and did not press "Apply Points".** *(CORRECTED by independent review 2026-11-17: the first version of this row claimed "on every order" and traced it through `clubPointFormField.blade.php` → `checkout_hide_field.blade.php` → `common/checkout/checkout_form.blade.php:125`. **Nothing renders that last file** — its only reference is LynomiaChat's ViewComposer naming it as a composer target — and the premise test asserting that chain could not fail. The real input is `clubPointApply.blade.php:25`, inside the eligibility gate at `:1-9`, reaching the browser via `CartController.php:114` → `checkout-cart-list.blade.php:97` → `checkout_list.blade.php:2` → `front-theme.js:224` into `.checkout_page_cart`, which sits between `Form::open` and `Form::close` at `checkout.blade.php:25/256/263`.)* It degenerates to **every** logged-in checkout when `checkout_points` and `checkout_price` are configured at `0`, which is savable because `RewardClubPointController:26-31` validates them with a bare `'required'`. A hand-crafted POST, or any storefront-API caller reaching `PaymentService.php:3435`, sets the flag regardless of what the form renders. The redeem branch does `$totalClubPoint = $orderClubPoint;` at `:39` — an **assignment, not `+=`** — and saves it at `:46-47`, so `customers.total_club_point` is REPLACED by just that order's earned points while a `reward_club_points` row records `used_points` = the entire prior balance. Separately `$requests_data['final_amount_point'] == $order['final_price']`, plainly meant to refuse a forged total, sits in the dead right operand. **Two further defects in the same file** (`:37` earned points, `:41` ledger `reward_price`) carry the `($a * $b ?? 0) / ($c ?? 0)` shape: measured inside the booted app as `DivisionByZeroError` on a missing or zero divisor and `ErrorException: Undefined array key` on a missing multiplicand, i.e. a fatal during order confirmation. All measured by executing the expressions, not by reading them. | **V1 CONTAINED 2026-11-17 by P5-12** under owner decision **D7**. `App\Listeners\NormaliseRewardRedemptionFlag` reduces the flag to `'on'`-or-absent before the vendored listener reads it — a shape normalisation, not a second decision, so it cannot drift from the ELEVEN first-party sites that apply the discount (eleven apply; nine dispatch -- `KhaltiPaymnetController:972` applies and dispatches nothing, a pre-existing leak already assigned to D4). Ordering measured: module providers arrive from `app->booted()`, and a probe registered that way observed the flag as `ABSENT`. 19 tests, 8/8 mutations redden. **V2 AND V3 ARE NOT CONTAINED AND CANNOT BE FIRST-PARTY** — both read `$setting` from `getAdminAllSetting()` rather than from the event, so no listener can reach them; risk recorded and containment proposed in `docs/security/P05-VENDOR-DEFECTS-REWARDCLUBPOINT.md`, which is also the upstream report (file, line, parse, truth table, minimal diff). **BALANCES ALREADY OVERWRITTEN ARE NOT REPAIRED**: D4 forbids retrospective balance changes without separate approval, and a ledger row cannot distinguish a real redemption from this defect because the `'off'` path wrote those rows too. **Severity is conditional on `D3`, which is unresolved** — whether `RewardClubPoint` is enabled in production is not answerable from this container (`add_on_managers` has zero rows; no WorkDo module loads). |

| `GAP-205` | `P04-P8-02` | 05 | **Inclusive-tax pricing disagrees between the guest and the signed-in cart, and P4-F070 changed what each one records.** `Cart::cart_list_cookie` adds `($is_inclusive_tax ? 0 : $tax_price)` to its total; `ApiController::cart_list` adds `$tax_price` to `total_sub_price` with **no inclusive exemption** (the two `$final_total = … + $tax_price` lines), and the cod / bank-transfer writer records that key as `orders.final_price`. | `GAP-213` | `ANA-006` | 05 | **Questions the independent review of the `/finance` ledger figures (P5-16) put to the owner, and what it found beyond them.** *(a) Does a single order the ledger never saw reset the verified period for good?* `LedgerCoverage` starts the period on the day after the **latest** order with no ledger row, and that is a reading of D1 ("state the start date"), not its text. Unrowed orders are not only history: `OrderObserver::created()` swallows every `\Exception` from `createForOrder()` (a missing currency rate is one) and logs a `critical`, and `syncFromOrder` returns at once when there is no row, so nothing ever writes it. One recent failure therefore moves the start forward permanently, and a store whose gap is **today** shows "Not available" until tomorrow. Repairing the row is a backfill, which D1 forbids. *(b) The fourth label.* D2 is recorded as *"Estimated gross profit with limitations"*; the tile is labelled **Estimated gross profit** and carries the limitations visibly beneath it (excludes shipping and tax; cost missing on N settled orders; the period). If "with limitations" was meant literally as part of the label, that is a one-line change in `resources/lang`. The Arabic is *إجمالي الربح التقديري*; the proposal had *الربح بعد تكلفة البضاعة*. *(c) Cash for an order the ledger never saw.* A cash-on-delivery order placed before the ledger existed (or lost to a swallowed failure) and collected afterwards reaches **neither the ledger nor the wallet nor either figure**: the period is set by order date and the figures are dated by payment, and `syncFromOrder` returns immediately without a row. Pre-existing; the tiles now say orders without a record are excluded *even if paid inside the period*; whether the wallet should be credited is a financial-policy decision of the same family as P4-F032. *Found by independent review of P5-16 and verified against the code.* **Added by a second, three-lens review of the fixes (2026-11-18) — all verified by reading or running, none a defect in a number:** *(d) The metric layer's own caveat is half untrue.* `MetricCaveat::EXCLUDES_SHIPPING_AND_TAX` ("Margin excludes shipping and tax") is attached to every `StoreRevenueMargin` answer, but for a store that enters prices **inclusive of tax** the tax is inside `product_price` and so inside the margin (`Cart.php` carries the tax-bearing line prices into `PaymentService`'s `$product_price`). The `/finance` tile no longer makes the claim (it now says shipping is not included and that tax is part of the sales value if prices include it); the caveat string and any report that prints it still do — fix with P5-3. *(e) A recurring gap shows `0.00` for the first hours after midnight.* A store whose ledger loses one order a day shows "not available" during day D and then `0.00 USD from D+1` from midnight until the next failed order moves the start again: correct (nothing has been paid in a period that has just begun) but easy to misread; not changed, because the alternatives (no window until it spans a full day) are product decisions tied to (a). *(f) Fourteen of the sixteen selectable languages show these tiles in English* (only `en` and `ar` carry the new strings; the date alone is localised), as roughly 1,300 other strings already do. *(g) The PHP default time zone is flipped to `Asia/Kolkata` inside model accessors* (`Product`, `ProductVariant`, `FlashSale`) and never restored, so `order_date` and `paid_at` carry the zone of the request that wrote them — traced to the storefront checkout; the effect on this feature is bounded and conservative (a verified start up to about a day late, never early) and the root cause is outside it. *(h) Cost caveat is a lower bound:* an order that mixes costed and uncosted lines has a non-zero cost and is never counted, so the tile now says *at least* N and *may be* overstated. | **OWNER.** Confirm or change (a)'s rule (alternatives: tolerate a gap older than N days, or list gaps individually); confirm the label in (b); decide (c) with the P5-FIN family. |
| `GAP-214` | `MKT-005` | 05 | **At a gateway return, the discount is priced from one customer and the points are taken from another.** `adoptStoredCheckoutState()` pins `customer_id` to the value stored at initiation, but the return handlers price the reward discount from `Customer::find($requests_data['customer_id'])` and write the order for `auth('customers')->id()` when someone is signed in at the return; the vendored listener debits the **order's** customer. A shopper who starts a checkout as customer A (an eligible balance, flag stored `'on'`) and completes the return signed in as customer B gets a discount priced from A's balance while B is debited, so **A's points are never spent and can be redeemed again**. The guest form of this (a guest who types a victim's e-mail, whose id the checkout adopts with no proof of ownership, and who carried a flag beside it) is closed at the source by D6 stage 1; the two-account form is not. *Read in code at five handlers — generic `getProductStatus`, Easebuzz, PaiementPro, CinetPay, AuthorizeNet — and not executed.* **Also recorded here, from the review of stage 1:** (i) a multipart file part can replace *any* field the checkout intake merges (`slug`, `store_id`, `payment_type`, …) because `Request::all()` is `array_replace_recursive(input, files)` — closed for `customer_id` only, at `processOrder()` and `whatsapp()`. The replaced value is the upload's temporary path, not a value the caller chooses, so the class is *make the request fail at a point of the caller's choosing*; that matters wherever a failure after a commit leaves a half-written state (it did, for rewards). `GuardsGatewayReturn::adoptStoredCheckoutState()` pins `customer_id` the same way (a merge) and is exposed to the same trick at the gateway returns — *reasoned from the mechanism, not executed*; (ii) the PartialPayments arm never evaluates the reward branch yet an eligible shopper's `'on'` still reached the event (balance replaced, no discount in the total — executed) — **fixed at initiation** (`prepareFinalRequestData()` withdraws the redemption from the stored state), which also covers the held generic gateway return's identical arm without touching it, for checkouts begun after this ships; (iii) a refusal is stored as `'off'`, which the vendored listener treats as truthy, so the COD and stored-state paths depend on `NormaliseRewardRedemptionFlag` being registered; (iv) sessions stored before a deploy hold an unsanitised `'on'`, re-checked at WhatsApp but not at the held returns; (v) a refusal is silent — the Blade can offer a discount the server then declines; (vi) **the debit is the last step of an unguarded post-commit tail** — the SMS module's `SendMsg` (an unguarded template dereference), `Utility::affiliateTransaction()` (a null-user dereference) and pre-order history run between the commit and the event, so a merchant's configuration alone can leave a committed, discounted order with no debit, no attacker needed; modules do not load in the suite, so no test sees it. The structural answer is D4's obligation record, or moving the debit ahead of that tail across nine writers — the owner's call. Also at the returns: quote and write are priced on different bases and can disagree in both directions; **Khalti** applies the discount and never dispatches the event (no debit, no ledger row); **NMI**'s shipping-ON arm never calls `applyTo`; **PayU**'s block is unrouted dead code. | **OWNER DECISION.** Four of these handlers are the ones D-13 / D-18 already hold for the `$plan` guard, so changing their reward block is not a small slice. Options: (a) refuse a redemption at the return unless the signed-in customer is the stored customer (changes no amount); (b) read and gate the signed-in customer's balance at the return, accepting a visible charge/total disagreement; (c) disable redemption on gateway flows until D4's obligation record exists. Recommendation: (a), then D4. Measurement and per-site rows: `docs/development/phase-5/PHASE-05-D6-WIRING-MEASUREMENT.md` §2 and §6. |
| `GAP-215` | `MKT-008` | 05 | **The opt-in records consent for an address nobody has proved the shopper owns.** The preference centre (`CommunicationPreferencesController`, D8-3b) is the one surface in the product allowed to write a `granted` event, and it is deliberately authenticated so that a person can only speak for themselves. That holds for the PERSON and not for the ADDRESS: the grant is written against `$customer->email` / `$customer->mobile`, and a shopper can put a stranger's details there. **Measured by reading both handlers of `AccountProfileController`:** (i) `mobile` has **no validation rule of any kind** in either `store()` (line 154) or the contact handler (line 198) — the rule arrays carry only `first_name` and `email` — so `$customer->mobile = $request->mobile` writes an arbitrary string, with no format check and no uniqueness, and two shoppers may hold the same number; (ii) `email` **is** validated (`required`, `email`, store-scoped `unique` excluding self), which stops one shopper taking another *registered* shopper's address in the same store but places no obstacle at all to a real address that is simply not registered there; (iii) **`customers.email_verified_at` exists and cannot serve as the signal, in both directions** — the only production writer is `CustomerController::store` (line 202), the MERCHANT creating a customer by hand, which stamps `now()` unconditionally, so where it is set it attests that a merchant typed an address rather than that anyone proved it; storefront self-registration never sets it at all, so it is NULL for exactly the population that uses this page; and `AccountProfileController` changes the e-mail without clearing it, so a merchant-created shopper who edits their own address keeps a stamp that now vouches for an unverified one. Consequence: a store can hold a consent record, with an IP, a user agent and a timestamp, for a third party who never asked for anything — which is worse than holding no record, because the record is what the store would rely on. No send is currently gated on this (`MarketingSendGate` is in `report` mode, enforcement off), so nothing is being mailed on the strength of it today. *Found by the independent tenant-authz review of D8-3 (finding F1, HIGH); every code claim above re-verified by reading the cited handlers.* | **OWNER DECISION — what makes a consent record count.** Engineering cannot settle it: a verification step is a product and legal choice, and the choices differ per channel. Options: (a) double opt-in — the tick records a *pending* state and only a click in a confirmation message to that address records `granted` (needs one new state in `ConsentState`, a confirmation token, and a decision on how long a pending grant lives); (b) grant only for an address the account has proved, which requires building a verification path first, since (iii) shows none exists; (c) accept a single opt-in as the record, and write the decision down so the basis is on file. Independent of the choice, (i) is a plain defect worth fixing either way: the mobile field should be validated. Recommendation: (a) for e-mail and (b)'s prerequisite work for phone, with (i) fixed now. Do **not** switch `MarketingSendGate` to `enforce` before this is answered — enforcement would start treating these records as authority to send. |


Seven belong to Phase 06, which has not started, so their absence is expected. **TEN belong to
Phases 03 and 04, which are declared engineering-complete** — and eight of those ten are Phase 04
Commerce. That is the finding: a closed phase whose owner-approved scope included requirements
nobody in this repository could read.
| `GAP-216` | `ANA-001`, `P05-14` | 05 | **The shared admin chrome was HTTP 500 for every principal who resolved no store, and it had been known and routed around rather than fixed.** `partision/header.blade.php:12` hands `getStoreById(getCurrentStore())` to `HomeController::getThemeUrl()`, which dereferenced `$store->slug` unconditionally.
