That value is NULL for a merchant between stores and for the platform operator — `getCurrentStore()` returns the hardcoded literal 1 for a `super admin`, and `getStoreById(1)` finds nothing when store 1 does not exist. `StoreFinanceController` measured and reported it twice (`:806`, `:1639`), routed its own refusals to `/dashboard`, and recorded in a comment that the dashboard was a 500 there too and that the chrome was not finance's to fix. `.github/known-test-outcomes.json` carried `ProfileTest::test_profile_page_is_displayed` as one of only two accepted Feature failures and named the exact remedy: 'needs a null-store guard at HomeController.php:610 (and :612, the same expression in the sibling branch of the same if/elseif)'. | **CLOSED 2026-11-20 by P5-7**, which is the first surface in this product designed to work for a principal with NO store and therefore the first that could not route around it. `getThemeUrl()` now returns null, which covers both cited branches by returning before either is reached — and returns BEFORE the settings reads, because each falls back to `getCurrentStore()` and continuing would hand the operator another tenant's `domains`/`subdomain` as their own storefront link. The header already rendered `{{ $theme_url ?? '#' }}`, so the fix makes an unreachable guard live rather than adding a new one. MEASURED BOTH WAYS: with the guard `ProfileTest::test_profile_page_is_displayed` passes, with it reverted it fails. The nine other files referencing the defect were run (109 passed) and the only test that reddened was `AddonMigrationStrandingTest::test_the_profile_page_failure_is_not_this_bug`, which existed to assert the 500 persisted and whose own message said to retire it if it ever went green — retired, with the manifest entry deleted and CLAUDE.md's count corrected, all in one commit as both the test and the manifest require. Pinned by `tests/Feature/Ui/StorelessChromeRendersTest.php` (4 tests, mutation-verified). |
| `GAP-217` | `ANA-007`, `P05-15` | 05 | **Two money tiles on the sales-summary card carry no disclosure at all, on the same measured basis as the four that now do.** `_sales_stats.blade.php` shows six tiles. `Order value` and `Order value, including shipping and tax` both carry a definition and, since 2026-11-21, the precision caveat. `Orders` and `Items ordered` are counts and correctly carry no money caveat. `Shipping charged` and `Coupon value applied` are MONEY and neither states its precision limit. **CORRECTED 2026-11-21, same day, by re-measuring the tiles one by one:** this row first said both carry "no hint at all", which is true only of `Shipping charged`. `Coupon value applied` DOES carry a definition — "The amount discounted by coupons, already deducted from order value." — and lacks only the caveat. So the work is one NEW hint and one caveat APPENDED to an existing one, and the `<x-ss.stat>` census moves 23 -> 24, not 23 -> 25. Both figures are accumulated exactly like the two tiles that now disclose: `$TotalShippingCharge += (float) $order->delivery_price` and `$TotalCouponAmount += (float) $order['coupon_price']` (ReportController :584-585, :652-653, and via `->sum()` at :733-734, :747-748), off FLOAT columns. So `MetricCaveat::FLOAT_PRECISION` is as true of them as of the tiles that state it. | **CLOSED 2026-11-21.** Both tiles now disclose, through `App\Support\Reports\ReportFigureNote` as this row's own next action required -- never a fresh literal, since one literal duplicated across four blades is what let two of them diverge in the first place. `Shipping charged` gained a definition AND the caveat (a NEW hint, measured from `$TotalShippingCharge += (float) $order->delivery_price`, and its second sentence is conditional because the arithmetic is: `SalesSummaryLabelTruthTest` verified that `orders.final_price` includes shipping only where the plan charges it). `Coupon value applied` kept its existing definition verbatim and gained the caveat. The `<x-ss.stat>` census moved **23 -> 24** in the same change, with the reason written into the test, and `SalesSummaryLabelTruthTest`'s coupon assertion was redirected to follow the data path rather than grep a literal that had moved -- the third time that repair was needed in that file, which is now recorded there. New pin: `ReportFigureNoteIsTheOnlySourceTest::test_every_money_hint_carries_the_precision_caveat()` enumerates the class's own `*Hint()` methods and fails both when a caveat is dropped from one AND when a new money figure is added without one -- the hint census counts hints, not their content, so nothing else would have caught either. Three mutations, three caught. **ONE SMALL REMAINDER, recorded here rather than as its own row:** `Orders` on that card still has no hint. It is a COUNT, so no money caveat applies and this row's subject is closed, but it has no DEFINITION either while the `/analytics` orders tile does ("Orders placed in this period, whatever their payment status..."). Adding one moves the census again, so it is a deliberate next step and not an oversight. |
| `GAP-218` | `MKT-001`, `P01-SEC-09` | 05 | **All five Marketing-menu screens gate on `isAbleTo()` alone, so they refuse the platform operator and refuse a tenant owner whose role does not carry the name.** Measured 2026-11-21 while building MKT-001's analytics link: `CouponController::index` via `couponReadRefusal()`, and a bare `auth()->user()->isAbleTo('Manage X')` in `NewsletterController:21`, `FlashSaleController:135`, `WishlistController:21` and `CartController::abandon_carts_handled:1153`. None has the operator-first string check on `users.type`, and none gives the tenant owner the `creatorId() === id` pass that `ReportPermission` and `StoreSettingsAccess::permits()` both give — and whose stated reason applies identically here: a merchant created before a permission name existed is locked out of their own screen. MEASURED, not inferred: a freshly created store owner with no role permissions gets **HTTP 302** from `/coupon`, which is how this was found (the first version of `MarketingLinksToAnalyticsTest` failed its own premise). | **OPEN. FAIL-CLOSED, so it is a consistency and availability gap and NOT a privilege escalation** — it refuses principals who should pass; it admits nobody who should not, and the data behind each screen is still scoped by `getCurrentStore()`. In production a merchant's seeded `admin` role carries these names, so an owner passes in practice and the operator does not. DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED IN MKT-001: changing owner-pass semantics on five live authorization gates is a behaviour change, not a link, and this project has already had a permission fix refuse the store owner against **two independent tests that encoded the old behaviour as the shipped contract** (P4-9b). Next action: decide whether the operator and owner passes belong on these five, and if so route all five through one predicate in `app/Services/` the way `ReportAccess` and `StoreSettingsAccess` already are, with the existing tests re-read first to see which behaviour they pin. `MarketingLinksToAnalyticsTest` documents the measurement and grants the five names explicitly, so it will keep passing either way. **CORRECTED 2026-11-22 BY A READ-ONLY MAPPING PASS WHOSE THREE ADVERSARIAL VERIFIERS ALL REFUTED THE ROW. Every correction below was then re-read by hand before being written here.** **(1) THE HEADLINE CLAIM IS FALSE FOR THE FIRST SITE.** `CouponController::couponReadRefusal()` does NOT gate on `isAbleTo()` alone: it carries the operator-first limb at `:221-224` (`$isOperator = UserPolicy::isOperator($user); if (! $isOperator && ! $user->isAbleTo('Manage Coupon'))`), plus a store-selection guard at `:227` and a store-resolution guard at `:232`. Its own docblock attributes this to an owner decision of **2026-10-28** — which PREDATES this row's 2026-11-21 measurement, so the row was stale the day it was written. The operator pass is pinned: `CouponOperatorRightsTest:70` asserts the operator gets **HTTP 200** from `/coupon`. On coupon only the OWNER limb is missing. **The real scope is four sites, not five.** **(2) ADMITTING THE OWNER IS NOT AN ENGINEERING CONSISTENCY FIX, AND THIS IS THE CORRECTION THAT MATTERS MOST.** `SavedViewPolicy:84` declares `OWNER_ADMITTED_SCREENS = ['analytics']`, and its docblock at `:55` states that **none of these five belongs there and that this is deliberate** — because a screen gated on `isAbleTo()` alone already refuses the owner, and admitting one would make SAVING a view more permissive than SEEING the list it describes. `SavedViewIsolationTest:219` pins the constant to exactly `['analytics']` by name. So the owner pass contradicts a recorded deliberate decision and a test that encodes it: **it is the owner's call, not engineering's**, and the earlier reading of this row (that `ReportAccess`'s owner pass 'applies identically here') is withdrawn. **(3) READ AND WRITE MUST STAY DISTINCT.** `FlashSaleTenancyTest:209` pins the platform operator being REFUSED a flash-sale WRITE, and does so non-vacuously — it grants `Edit Flash Sale` first precisely so the operator limb is what the assertion measures. Any operator pass therefore goes on READ doors only; `FlashSaleController` already distinguishes them (`flashSaleWriteRefusal():81-89` refuses the operator deliberately). **(4) THE CORRECT SHAPE IS ALREADY IN ONE OF THE FIVE FILES AND `index()` SIMPLY DOES NOT CALL IT.** `FlashSaleController::flashSaleReadRefusal():52-74` is the operator-first predicate, and `create():179` calls it while `index():135` kept the bare one-line form. **(5) THE SITE COUNT IS LOW BECAUSE ONLY INDEX SCREENS WERE COUNTED.** Detail screens behind them gate bare too — e.g. `CouponController::show():339` renders `coupon.show` behind `isAbleTo('Create Coupon')`, a CREATE permission guarding a READ screen, linked from the eye icon on every coupon row (`coupon/action.blade.php:3`). **(6) `CartController::abandon_carts_handled` is at `:1187`, not `:1153`** — independently confirmed; the method opens at `:1185`. It is also the only one of the set with no `auth()->user() &&` null guard, relying on route `Authenticate`. **(7) TWO MORE ESTABLISHED PREDICATES EXIST INSIDE MARKETING ITSELF**, as PRIVATE COPIES rather than services (`RecoveryQueueController` among them), so a shared predicate would consolidate more than the three named above. **DONE 2026-11-22 for the operator half.** `App\Services\MarketingScreenAccess::refusal()` extracts the predicate coupon and flash-sale already proved, and the four READ doors call it; `flashSaleReadRefusal()` now delegates so one decision lives in one place. The OWNER pass is NOT implemented and remains the owner's, because `SavedViewPolicy` decides it the other way. No write gate moved. 25 tests, 10 mutations, all caught. **THE TENANT-AUTHZ REVIEW OF THAT CHANGE FOUND FOUR THINGS, all addressed:** (i) it created an asymmetry — `NewsletterController::fileExport()` kept a bare `isAbleTo()` and its docblock justified that with "anyone who can SEE the button already holds the permission", a premise the operator's admission destroyed, since the export button is gated on the module and plan rather than a permission; both doors now share the predicate. (ii) the first test set proved ADMISSION and never ISOLATION — dropping `where('store_id', getCurrentStore())` from any of the four DataTables left every test green; a two-tenant test now drives the real XHR leg and asserts the other tenant's row is absent. (iii) no test pinned the refusal SHAPE (302 + flashed `error`); one does now. (iv) a test of mine asserted the wrong mechanism: `current_store = 0` does NOT make a merchant's store unresolvable, because 0 is `empty()` and `getCurrentStore()` (`helper.php:869-878`) falls through to `Store::where('created_by', $user->id)`. The limb bites only for a principal who owns no store AND has none selected. **(8) OPEN — A SCOPE STATEMENT FOR THE OWNER, NOT A DEFECT, AND DELIBERATELY NOT DECIDED HERE.** yajra dispatches `?action=csv|excel|pdf` through the SAME controller method as the list, so admitting the operator to these three screens also admits them to the DOWNLOAD of subscriber e-mail addresses, and of wishlist and abandoned-cart shoppers' names and e-mail addresses. That follows from the 2026-10-28 decision that the operator may read and export — but that decision was taken about COUPONS, which are commercial settings, and these are SHOPPER PERSONAL DATA. The payload class differs, and consent/privacy policy is explicitly not engineering's to invent. It stays fail-closed per selected store (no store chosen -> refused). **The owner should confirm whether operator export of shopper personal data is intended; if not, the narrowing is one predicate on the export leg and no list access needs to change.** |
| `GAP-219` | `MKT-009`, `MKT-007` | 05 | **The four first-party shopper-nudge endpoints keep no record that a nudge was sent and carry no throttle, so the same shopper can be messaged the same thing repeatedly.** Measured 2026-11-21 from `php artisan route:list`: `POST abandon-carts-messagesend`, `POST abandon-carts-emailsend`, `POST abandon-wishlist-message-send` and `POST abandon-wish-emailsend` all carry `web, Authenticate, XSS, SetLocate, EmailIsVerified` and **no `throttle`**, and neither `AbandonedMessageDispatch` nor the two controllers write any `sent_at` / `last_sent` / notified marker — `blockedReason()` asks five questions (mobile present, WhatsApp configured, template exists, template active, marketing consent) and none of them is "have we already sent this". `CartController::abandon_carts_messsend`'s own docblock already records that "the endpoint has no rate limit of its own", written when the nudge was made deliverable for the first time. So one merchant pressing the button twice sends twice. | **OPEN. This is the duplication that is actually LIVE, and it is NOT the one `MKT-009` describes** — see that row for why the Lynomia-versus-platform duplication cannot occur today. Two parts, and only the first is engineering's: (1) a technical double-submit/throttle guard on four outbound endpoints is ordinary correctness and a cost control (each click is a billable WhatsApp message), in the same family as P4-3b's double-submit work; (2) **how often a shopper may be nudged is a MARKETING POLICY and therefore the owner's** — a window length is a business rule, and the standing instruction is not to invent one. Next action: put the bounded technical option (reject an identical send for the same shopper and template within a short idempotency window, recorded, refusal reported to the merchant as such) to the owner together with the policy question, rather than shipping a number nobody chose. **CORRECTED 2026-11-22 — THE ENGINEERING HALF IS BUILT AND THIS ROW WAS STALE, which is the `GAP-229` pattern again: the row describes the code as it was when the row was written, and nothing re-read it afterwards.** Measured by mapping every `NudgeGate::reserve()` call to the method that contains it: **all four endpoints reserve** — `CartController::abandon_carts_emailsend` (:1369), `CartController::abandon_carts_messsend` (:1583), `WishlistController::abandon_wish_emailsend` (:249) and `WishlistController::abandonWishlistMsgSend` (:698). The record the row says does not exist is `marketing_nudge_sends` via `App\Models\MarketingNudgeSend`, deliberately NOT `Cachable` because a cached miss on "did this already happen" is a second billable message; `NudgeThrottle` and `NudgeDecision` carry the decision and `NudgeIdempotencyTest` / `NudgeLedgerReadsTest` pin it. So part (1), the technical double-submit guard, is **DONE**. **Part (2) is unchanged and remains the owner's**: how often a shopper may be nudged is marketing policy, and no window has been invented. **ALSO MEASURED AND NOT A DEFECT, recorded so the next reader does not re-open it:** `POST abandon-wish-emailsend` is declared TWICE in `routes/web.php` (:1290 and :1308). CLAUDE.md warns that a later declaration replaces an earlier one and silently drops its middleware — here both declarations carry the same handler, the same route name and the same middleware, and `route:list` resolves one route with `web, Authenticate, XSS, SetLocate, EmailIsVerified` intact. It is dead duplication, not a dropped guard. |
| `GAP-230` | `ANA-TOP`, `P5-R14` | 04 Commerce / 05 | **MEASURED, NOT FIXED: three admin-API callers stamp `orders.currency_code` through a reader that can answer for the VIEWER's store rather than the order's.** `OrderCurrencyStamp::codeFor($storeId)` reads `Utility::GetValueByName('defult_currancy', $storeId) ?? GetValueByName('currency', $storeId)`, and that helper falls through on a miss to `Utility::Setting()`, which **ignores its store-id argument whenever `Auth::check()` is true** and reads `getCurrentStore()` instead (`app/Models/Utility.php:139-149`, reached from `:228-231`). For the three writers that pass their own store this is harmless — the fallback resolves the same store. It is not harmless for `Api/DashboardController.php:6879` and `Api/ApiController.php:3383,9048`, which resolve the store from the request (`resolveOwnedStore`) while `CustomAuth` has logged a `User` token into the `web` guard, so `Auth::check()` is true: a multi-store merchant creating an order on store B while their `current_store` is A would have B's order stamped with **A's** currency whenever B has no `defult_currancy`/`currency` row of its own. **LATENT, NOT LIVE, on data this product created:** `StoreController:153-158,310-315` seeds `defult_currancy => 'USD'` on every store made through the product, so only a store created outside that path, or one whose rows were deleted, reaches the fallback. Found by the independent review of ANA-TOP part 3, which had moved a READ onto the same helper; that read was corrected to an explicitly scoped query (`OrderCurrencyStamp::configuredCodeFor()`), and this row is the WRITE half, deliberately left alone. | **OPEN — not engineering's to decide alone.** `codeFor()` decides what currency a new order is recorded in, so changing it changes recorded financial data for future orders, which the class's own docblock already flags as the owner's call (`D-N34` covers rewriting orders already written). The bounded fix is one line — route `codeFor()` through the scoped reader and fall back to `DEFAULT_CODE` instead of to the viewer's store — and it is written down rather than applied, with the reachability above, so the owner decides on evidence. A regression test exists for the read half (`OrderCurrencyStampConfiguredReaderTest`); the write half needs a two-store admin-API case, which should land with the fix, not before it. |
| `GAP-220` | `MKT-010` | 03 / 05 | **CLOSED for the structural half, 2026-11-21.** The owner's instruction of the same day ("implement an accessible, responsive, properly styled conversion-action overlay across all three themes, using the approved design system") answered the open question — where the styling lives — so the work proceeded. **THE DIAGNOSIS WAS RIGHT AND THE PROPOSED REMEDY WAS WRONG.** `supdiv` really did occur in exactly one file in the repository, and the buttons really were unstyled. But the remedy "add greentic's missing call, then style the overlay" assumed the overlay needed positioning. Reading the themes refuted that: **techzonix already places the hook inside `.pro-btn-wrapper`, which is `absolute top-4 right-4`, and stylique inside its own `absolute top-4 right-4` container** — so the float already existed in theme markup, and a first draft giving the parent `position: relative` through `:has()` would have overridden their `absolute` and dropped the buttons out of the corner. The overlay was never the missing part; the WRAPPER's styling was. **What shipped:** the hook now carries `ss-product-actions` (keeping `supdiv` for anything outside this repository), its inert inline `top: 40px` is gone, and `store-syria-ds.css` — loaded by all three theme layouts via `front_end/partials/appearance` — styles it with tokens only, a 44px WCAG 2.5.5 minimum target and a visible focus ring. **Position-agnostic by design**, so one rule set serves three themes that place the hook differently: that IS the parity. **Nothing is hidden until hover** — the draft's `@media (hover: hover)` fade was removed, because a keyboard user never hovers and a touch device has no hover state, so a conversion action gated on hover is unreachable for both. greentic's product card gained the call it never had (its shoppers had no quick view or compare at all), and **techzonix's call was moved out of the product `<a>`** — the module's own links and buttons were interactive content inside a link: invalid HTML, and activating one could follow the outer product link instead. `ProductActionOverlayTest` pins all seven properties; 8 mutations, all caught. | **OPEN, narrowed to two things and neither is engineering's to decide alone.** (1) **THE FOUR-COMBINATION VISUAL CAPTURE** (AR/EN × light/dark) has not been taken. The suite cannot substitute: the buttons come from `ProductQuickView`, `ProductCompare`, `ProductsPDF` and `ProductCSV`, which per CLAUDE.md load in **no test run** unless an `add_on_managers` row is inserted — so `ProductActionOverlayTest` is explicit that it proves every structural precondition and not that a button renders. (2) **ALL THREE THEMES USE `right-4`, A PHYSICAL UTILITY, on the container that positions these actions, so the overlay does NOT mirror in Arabic.** greentic's new container matches it deliberately: using `end-4` in one theme out of three would have made greentic the only mirroring theme, which is a different layout rather than a fix. Correcting it is a three-theme RTL change with its own capture, recorded here rather than done in a third of the themes. |
| `GAP-221` | `ANA-002` | 05 | **The twenty-four `ReportController` screens have no period filter, no comparison and no saved periods, so `/analytics` can now be filtered and none of the report screens beside it can.** Measured 2026-11-21: of the 24 `resources/views/reports/*.blade.php`, **0** contain `type="date"` or any date-range control. ANA-002's three capabilities (comparisons, filters, saved periods) were delivered on the merchant analytics centre, which is one screen; the reporting centre the same requirement covers is untouched. The building blocks are already first-party and reusable: `App\Support\Analytics\AnalyticsPeriod` resolves and clamps a period, `MetricWindow::previous()` gives the baseline, and `SavedView` + `TableFilterMemory::vocabularyFor()` store a named filter set per employee per store with no migration. | **OPEN, and bounded by something real rather than by effort.** `ReportController` is 3,660 lines and **most of its handlers cannot run under the suite at all** — 37 `MONTH(` and 10 `YEAR(` in `selectRaw`, absent from SQLite, so those handlers answer HTTP 500 in a Feature test. Adding a date filter to a handler no test can execute would be an unverifiable change to a live reporting surface, which is how the 18-site date-boundary bug survived 9,112 tests. The two handlers measured to hold **0** MySQL-only constructs (`order_category_reports` 1893-2360 and `order_brand_reports` 2382-2903) ARE suite-reachable and are therefore the only two that can be filtered with real test evidence today. Next action, in order: filter those two through `AnalyticsPeriod` with Feature tests; then either stand up the MySQL harness (`tests/Support/Security/bin/verify-sec13-migration-on-mysql.php` is the pattern) for the rest, or replace their `selectRaw` date grouping with Eloquent so the suite can reach them — the second is the larger change and the one that removes the blindness permanently. **CORRECTED 2026-11-22 — THE CLAIM AND THE NEXT ACTION WERE BOTH WRONG, AND THE CAUSE IS `GAP-229` FOR THE THIRD TIME: A CENSUS BY STRING.** The claim rests on "of the 24 `resources/views/reports/*.blade.php`, **0** contain `type="date"` or any date-range control". The grep is literally true and substantively false: **6 of the 25 report views carry `<x-ss.report-filter>` and post `chart_data` + `Date`** — `index`, `order_report`, `bar_chart_order_report`, `sale_by_category`, `sale_by_brand`, `sale_by_product`. They use a JS range picker, so they contain no `type="date"` anywhere. `PROJECT-STATE.md` already recorded this exact correction for `ANA-002` on 2026-11-22; it was applied to the traceability matrix and NOT to this row, so one claim was corrected in one document and left standing in the other. **THE STATED NEXT ACTION WOULD HAVE BUILT A FILTER ONTO TWO SCREENS THAT ALREADY HAVE ONE.** `order_category_reports` and `order_brand_reports` render `sale_by_category` and `sale_by_brand`, both already filtered; the handlers already branch on `chart_data` (`last-month` / `this-month` / `seven-day` / `year`) and parse a custom `$request->Date` range on `' to '`. **THE CITED LINE RANGES ARE ALSO STALE.** The file is **4,242** lines, not 3,660; `order_category_reports` is at `:2218-2684` and `order_brand_reports` at `:2722-3248`, not 1893-2360 and 2382-2903. Measured by brace balance, both still hold **0** MySQL-only constructs, so that conclusion survives — but the stale range for the first overlaps a DIFFERENT method and shows one `MONTH(`, which is exactly the trap CLAUDE.md records as "check your handler's own range before concluding it is untestable". **WHAT IS ACTUALLY LEFT, measured rather than counted.** 19 views lack the shared filter, but that number overstates the work in two ways. (a) Six are AJAX FRAGMENTS of already-filtered parents (`order_category_chart`, `order_brand_chart`, `order_chart_data`, `order_bar_chart_data`, `order_product_chart`, `chart_data`) and one is a partial (`_sales_stats`); a fragment has no filter because its parent carries it. (b) **The stock reports have no date dimension to filter.** `low_stock_*`, `most_stock_*`, `out_stock_*` and `stock_report` are point-in-time inventory states; a period filter over them would need historical stock snapshots, which this schema does not keep, and adding one would be inventing data rather than filtering it. **So the genuinely filterable, currently unfiltered, SUITE-REACHABLE screens are a short list:** `orderStatusReport` (`showOrderStatusReport:3497-3534`), `order_country_Report` (`showCountryOrderReport:3536-3793`), `top_5_reports` (`top_product:3275-3387`), `downloadable_product_report` (`OrderDownlodableReport:2709-2712`) and `finance_report` (`financeReport:746-772`) — five, each with 0 MySQL-only constructs, not twenty-four. The remaining unreachable handlers (`reports_chart` 21, `order_reports_chart_data` 16, `order_reports_chart` 6, `order_product_reports` 2) keep the original disposition: a MySQL harness or an Eloquent rewrite, and the second is still the change that removes the blindness permanently. **ONE OF THE FIVE IS NOW DONE (2026-11-22): `orderStatusReport`.** `showOrderStatusReport()` counted ALL TIME and said so in a hardcoded `periodLabel` -- a placeholder naming the limitation without removing it. It now accepts a period and the screen offers a control for it. **THE PERIOD IS OPT-IN, deliberately.** `AnalyticsPeriod::fromRequest([])` falls through to `DEFAULT` (`last_30_days`), so resolving unconditionally would have silently narrowed every existing reader's figure to 30 days without anyone asking -- the same number quietly answering a different question. With no `ss_period` the screen answers exactly as before, and a mutation proves it. **IT USES `AnalyticsPeriod`, NOT THE LEGACY `chart_data` VOCABULARY, and that is a recorded choice rather than an oversight.** The six already-filtered screens post `chart_data=last-month|...` with a `Date` range parsed on `' to '`; `/analytics` posts `ss_period` through `AnalyticsPeriod`, which clamps the span, labels it and yields a baseline. This row names `AnalyticsPeriod` as the building block and it is the one with tests and a clamp, so the reports centre briefly carries two period vocabularies -- CONVERGENCE on the newer one, not a third invention. **The legacy six are what should migrate; do not 'fix' this screen by reverting it to `chart_data`.** A PLAIN GET FORM, NO JAVASCRIPT, because this page renders server-side and has no AJAX fragment -- which also makes a filtered view bookmarkable and keeps it working with scripting off. The control sits OUTSIDE `#printableArea`: a printed report should carry its period as a caption, which the chart already does. EVIDENCE: 5 tests, 6 mutations, 6 caught. Two of those mutations are worth naming because both describe mistakes made and corrected while building this: imposing a default period on an unasked-for render, and **deleting the `All time` option while the test still passed** -- the words appear in the chart caption too, so the assertion had to be moved onto the `<option>` element. **A SECOND IS NOW DONE (2026-11-22): `order_country_Report`.** Same placeholder, one extra hazard that is the reason this screen needed its own test rather than a copy of the last one: it prints TWO numbers from TWO separate queries side by side -- `$withCountry` off the `order_billing_details`->`countries`->`orders` join, `$total` off a plain `Order::count()` -- in one footer reading "Orders with a billing country: X of Y total orders". Windowing the join and not the count would have put a February chart under an all-time total and told the merchant their orders had LOST their billing country. **That is the exact confusion this screen was already fixed for once** (two numbers counting different things printed side by side), reintroduced through a different door, so the window lands on both queries and one test holds them together. EVIDENCE: 7 tests (5 failed first), **8 mutations, 8 caught**, Reports+Analytics regression 503 passed, `tests/Unit/Ui` 1,112 passed. Two of the eight are worth naming. **S6 dropped only the UPPER bound of the range and survived all seven tests**, because every "outside the window" fixture in the file first sat BEFORE the window -- half the range had nothing asking about it; the fixtures gained a later-dated order and it is caught. **S4 could not be applied at all**, because the two handlers' view-data blocks are now byte-identical and the harness refuses an anchor matching twice -- the guard working, not a pass. **THE FORM IS NOW A SHARED COMPONENT, EXTRACTED AT THE SECOND COPY AND NOT THE FIFTH:** `<x-ss.analytics-period-filter>`. The cautionary tale is in the same directory -- `<x-ss.report-filter>` was hand-copied onto four screens and carried the same four accessibility defects into every copy, so fixing one screen fixed one screen. The two are NOT interchangeable and the component's docblock says so: that one posts the legacy `chart_data` and drives an AJAX fragment, this one posts `ss_period` through `AnalyticsPeriod`. `orderStatusReport` was moved onto it in the same change, and its own page-level test is what proved the refactor dropped nothing. **THE REMAINING THREE WERE THEN RE-MEASURED BY EXECUTION, AND THE LIST OF FIVE WAS ITSELF WRONG -- `GAP-229` A FOURTH TIME, AND THIS TIME IT IS MY OWN CENSUS.** The three line ranges are accurate (`top_product:3275-3387`, `OrderDownlodableReport:2709-2712`, `financeReport:746-772`; it was the category/brand ranges that were stale). The SUITE-REACHABILITY claim is not. **`top_product` answers an exception, not a page:** `SQLSTATE[HY000]: General error: 1 no such function: SUBSTRING_INDEX`. Its category and brand charts parse the comma-separated `orders.product_id` with `SUBSTRING_INDEX(SUBSTRING_INDEX(...))` across a generated-numbers cross join. The census behind the five grepped for `MONTH(`, `YEAR(`, `DATE_FORMAT`, `DAYNAME`, `MONTHNAME` and `STR_TO_DATE` -- the list CLAUDE.md names -- and `SUBSTRING_INDEX` is not on it, so a MySQL-only handler was classified as reachable **because the string I searched for was not the string that breaks it.** That is the same failure as the `type="date"` grep three paragraphs up, committed while correcting it. **Measured by driving the route, not by grepping: `top-all-reports` throws; `order-downloadable-reports` answers 200; `finance-reports` answers its permission refusal (`View Finance Dashboard`), so it is reachable with the right principal.** `top_product` therefore joins the MySQL-harness disposition with the other unreachable handlers and is NOT buildable with suite evidence today. **TWO REMAIN BUILDABLE, and both need a prerequisite checked before any code is written, because neither is the same shape as the two done.** (i) `downloadable_product_report` has NO query and NO `periodLabel` of its own -- it is four lines rendering `SalesDownloadableProductDataTable`, so the period belongs on the DataTable's `query()`, which is yajra-driven and AJAX-paged. **The GET-form pattern DOES transfer, and the reason is worth knowing rather than re-deriving: yajra sets the table's `ajax.url` to `url()->full()` as evaluated when the page RENDERS**, so reloading with `?ss_period=...` makes the table's own draw carry the same query string -- the fact `ProductController::index` and the MKT-002 filter-memory work both turn on. A client-side fill would leave the draw requesting the UNFILTERED url: the page would look filtered and the rows would not be, so both legs must be driven by any test. **It windows `orders.order_date`, not the `created_at` the table displays**, because every other screen in this centre windows `order_date` and the merchant's own order list shows it under the title "Date" (`OrderDataTable:840`); a period meaning a different thing on one screen of one centre is the defect this workstream removes. The two coincide today -- measured: all twelve `order_date` write sites set it to `now()` in the request that inserts the row and nothing edits it afterwards -- so no row changes membership. **TWO LEADS, recorded and not fixed inside a filter slice:** that DataTable scopes on `getCurrentStore()` rather than `OperatorStoreSelection::reportStoreId()`, unlike every handler around it; and its visible date column is `created_at` titled "Timestamp" while the window and the rest of the centre use `order_date` -- harmless while the two coincide, confusing the moment they do not. **DONE 2026-11-22: 6 tests, 6 mutations caught. AND IT COULD NOT BE TESTED AT ALL UNTIL A SEPARATE CRASH WAS FIXED, which the fixture found rather than a census.** Both of this table's column formatters ran `foreach (json_decode($order->product_json, true) ...)`, and `orders.product_json` is NULLABLE, so `foreach` was handed null and threw. **yajra formats inside the draw and catches at the top, so the blast radius was the WHOLE TABLE, not the row:** the response became `{"recordsTotal":2,"recordsFiltered":0,"data":[],"error":"foreach() argument must be of type array|object, null given"}` -- every healthy order replaced by a raw PHP message, with a count contradicting the empty list. The neighbouring case was already anticipated (`if (! is_array($product)) continue;` guards a malformed ELEMENT); the guard on the array itself was missing. Fixed in both formatters with 4 data-driven tests (null, empty string, non-JSON, JSON scalar) and 2 more mutations, and an order with nothing to list now formats as empty rather than being dropped. **SO FOUR OF GAP-221'S SCREENS ARE DONE AND THE FIFTH IS NOT BUILDABLE:** `top_product` keeps the MySQL-harness disposition. (ii) `finance_report` has no `periodLabel` either, and its figures come from `FinanceLedgerFigures::forStore()` and `WalletEntitlementFigure::forStore()`. **Some of those are BALANCES, not flows** -- "available to withdraw" is a position at an instant, and a date range over it is as meaningless as one over a stock level, which is the argument this row already uses to exclude the stock reports. **THAT PREREQUISITE WAS THEN ESTABLISHED, AND IT SETTLED BOTH WAYS AT ONCE (2026-11-22), SO `finance_report` IS ALSO DONE.** The two ledger tiles are FLOWS -- `CollectedCustomerPayments` is "money customers actually paid, counted on the day it was paid" and `StoreRevenueMargin` is dated the same way -- so a window narrows them honestly. The wallet tile is the balance, its assembler takes no window at all, and its own caption already reads `right now`. **And the screen was MORE period-aware than its missing label suggested: `FinanceLedgerFigures::forStore()` has accepted a `?MetricWindow` and a label since D1 Option A** -- it clamps the request to the ledger's VERIFIED coverage and refuses, in words with a date, a period ending before the records begin. The handler simply never passed one. So this slice wires an existing capability to a control and re-implements none of the clamp: a second copy of that rule is how two screens start disagreeing about the same money. EVIDENCE: 8 tests, **6 mutations, 6 caught -- but FOUR SURVIVED THE FIRST ROUND and every one was a vacuous assertion of mine, which is the part worth recording.** (1) The tile prints a SUM, not a list, so putting one payment inside the window and two outside and then searching the page for the individual amounts passed even with the window removed entirely: the all-time figure `11,100.00` does not contain `4,000.00` and does contain `100.00`. (2) Checking only that the UNFILTERED caption was absent let a mutation pass the window while dropping the label. (3) Asserting the words `right now` appeared *somewhere* let a mutation relabel the balance tile with the chosen period -- the exact "make it consistent" change the code comment warns against. (4) Both flow tiles carry the same refusal text, so dropping the hint from ONE left the other printing it and the search was satisfied by the wrong element. All four now compare the tile's own value through `viewData()`, or count occurrences, instead of searching the page. **2026-11-25 — THE FIFTH SCREEN IS DONE AND, MORE IMPORTANTLY, IS NOW SUITE-REACHABLE AT ALL.** `top_product()`'s category and brand rankings joined `orders.product_id` against a generated 1..100 series with `SUBSTRING_INDEX`, so the screen answered HTTP **500** under the Feature suite and had no executable evidence — this row's own disposition called removing that blindness "the one that removes it permanently". 8 of 8 failed first with `no such function: SUBSTRING_INDEX`; 12 pass now (metric, ranking, five-row limit, name tie-break, case/space fold, the 101st-position case, and THREE separate store predicates), plus 7 more for the period filter. **THE FIGURES WERE PROVED UNCHANGED ON THE PRODUCTION ENGINE BEFORE THE REWRITE WAS TRUSTED** — `verify-top-five-reports-on-mysql.php`, 19 checks, 0 failed, returning exactly the values it was written against while the old SQL was live. 8 + 9 mutations, 16 caught; **the one survivor is recorded in the class rather than hidden** (one order listing a product twice counts once is held twice over, so mutating either site alone changes no figure). One behaviour difference stated rather than left to be found: the old series stopped at the 100th element of `orders.product_id` and silently omitted the rest; the rewrite reads the whole list, towards the documented definition, and a test pins it. **The four remaining handlers keep the MySQL-harness disposition** (`reports_chart` 21 MySQL-only constructs, `order_reports_chart_data` 16, `order_reports_chart` 6, `order_product_reports` 2). **And `KnownUnrenderableRoutesTest`'s docblock listing `/top-all-reports  no such function: SUBSTRING_INDEX` as an environment-only 500 is now corrected** — it was prose no assertion depended on, which is why it went stale unnoticed. |
| `GAP-222` | `ANA-005`, `P5-FIN-4` | 05 | **The settlement figure — the third of owner decision D2's four — is the only one that does not go through the metric layer, and it is printed with no currency on it.** Measured 2026-11-21: `resources/views/finance/index.blade.php:95` renders `{{ number_format($wallet->available_balance, 2) }}` under *Available to withdraw*, a bare decimal with no currency code and no period. Four sibling wallet tiles on the same card do the same (*Pending Withdrawals*, *Pending Balance*, *Total Income*, *Total Withdrawn*). Meanwhile `App\Support\Metrics\MerchantWalletEntitlement` exists **specifically** because the wallet's currency can differ from the order's — it selects `store_wallets.currency_code` and reports `COUNT(DISTINCT store_wallet_transactions.currency_code)` so a multi-currency wallet ledger is stated as its own fact rather than silently summed — and a repository-wide grep finds it referenced **nowhere outside `app/Support/Metrics/`**. `NetFinancialReceipts` is in the same position. So the one class written to disclose the currency hazard reaches no screen, while the screen shows the number without it. The labelling proposal's §2.3 required exactly this caveat: "the wallet currency when it differs from the store's display currency". | **CLOSED 2026-11-21.** All FIVE wallet figures now go through `App\Support\Metrics\WalletEntitlementFigure` and render as `<x-ss.stat>`, so each carries its currency code, a stated period and -- for the entitlement -- a visible caveat. The four siblings moved in the same change rather than being declared out of scope, because leaving them as bare decimals beside one corrected tile would have been the same defect four more times on the same card. **NOTHING IS CONVERTED AND NO RATE IS INVENTED**: when the wallet currency differs from the store's display currency the tile says so and both codes stand; when a store's wallets span more than one currency every figure refuses a total and the amounts are listed, the same refusal `FinanceLedgerFigures` makes. D2's mandated sentence *A running balance, not revenue.* is kept and is now asserted against THAT TILE's hint rather than against the page. The date inputs on `/finance` are stated as not applying, and a test proves the value does not move when they are set -- never applied to `updated_at`. **THE ZERO TRAP WAS REAL AND IS THE REASON `MoneyText::buckets()` TAKES A FLAG.** Reusing `realBuckets()` unchanged drops any bucket under half a cent, which is right for a ledger and wrong for a wallet: it would have dropped the ONLY bucket of an empty wallet, let the `?? $storeCurrency` fallback answer, and printed `0.00 USD` against a balance denominated in SYP -- the wrong code on the commonest wallet there is. Both callers' choices are written out at the one definition. **EVIDENCE:** `FinanceLedgerFiguresTest` 50 tests / 426 assertions (12 new); **16 mutations, all CAUGHT** -- M6 (dropping the tenant predicate from the raw sibling read) SURVIVED first and was a real coverage hole: the decoy tenant's four sibling columns were zero, so summing across tenants gave the right answer by accident, and the balance tile goes through `MetricScope`. The decoy now carries all five columns in a third currency. Raw-query censuses updated (`store_wallets` 1 -> 2 sites, 0 unscoped; 193 -> 194 in app/; totals 186 -> 187 and 238 -> 239); `tests/Feature/Tenancy/` 451 green; `StatHintIsVisibleTest` 25 -> 26 hints and its method renamed off the stale number; Unit and Contract green; 6 new strings in both language catalogues. **NOT in this change and still open:** `/finance` carries no *Order value*, so no single screen yet shows all four D2 figures -- that stays on the `ANA-005` row. |
| `GAP-223` | `ANA-005`, `ANA-007`, `P05-15` | 05 | **Two pre-existing defects on the order-report sales card, both found by the authorization review of ANA-005 rather than by any test, and both affecting ALL the tiles on that card.** (a) **THE PERIOD LABEL AND THE FIGURES DISAGREE ON 33 DAYS IN EVERY FIVE YEARS.** `ReportController::order_reports_chart` derives its `last-month` window with `strtotime('-1 month')` (`:646-648`), which overflows when today's day-of-month does not exist in the previous month -- 31 March minus one month is 3 March, so the window lands in the CURRENT month. `chartPeriodLabels()` (`:424`) uses the correct `'first day of last month'` for the LABEL. So on those days the card reads "Last month (February 2026)" over March figures. MEASURED, not estimated: evaluating both expressions for every day from 2024-01-01 to 2028-12-31 (1,827 days, two leap years) gives **33 overflow days**, and the detector `(int) date('j') > (int) date('t', strtotime('first day of last month'))` is exact on every one. (b) **A PLATFORM OPERATOR WHO HAS CHOSEN NO STORE IS SHOWN STORE 1's ORDER VALUE.** `getCurrentStore()` returns the hard-coded literal 1 for a `super admin` with no `current_store` (`helper.php:873`), and the six demand tiles pass it straight into `Order::where('store_id', getCurrentStore())` (`ReportController:725, 871, 947, 1464`). Store 1 is an ordinary merchant's tenant -- owner decision 7's own words, at `StoreFinanceController:833`, are that "store 1 belongs to a merchant who did not ask for it" -- and `/finance` refuses this exact principal at eight call sites before computing anything. | **(a) CLOSED 2026-11-21. (b) STILL OPEN AND OWNER-BLOCKED.** **(a) WAS FIVE TIMES LARGER AND ONE CLASS WORSE THAN THIS ROW RECORDED, and the correction is the point.** The row said one expression at `:646-648`. A mapped census found **NINE** overflowing derivations in `ReportController` -- `strtotime('-1 month')` at the window sites AND `Carbon::now()->subMonth()`, which overflows identically -- against **three** `subMonthNoOverflow()` calls that were ALREADY CORRECT and sat in the same methods. That juxtaposition is the evidence it was an oversight rather than a decision. **THREE MEASURED CONSEQUENCES, not one.** (i) the card named a month its figures did not cover; (ii) **THE CHART SILENTLY DROPPED ORDERS** -- the window returned 31 days of March while the already-correct loop bound ran 1..28, so rows dated 29, 30 and 31 were never read into the series, which is data loss and not a labelling error; (iii) `order_reports_chart_data` paired the overflowing `$lastMonth` with the correct `$lastDayofMonth` in a `whereBetween`, giving `['2026-03-03', '2026-02-28']` -- a **reversed range matching no row**, so that figure read **ZERO**, the one wrong answer a merchant cannot tell apart from a quiet month. Two further sites the original row did not know about: `getLastMonthDatesFormatted()`, the x-axis label generator for **six** surfaces, emitted 31 labels beginning `03-Mar` under a February heading; and `$date = (int) date('j', strtotime($end))` was **dead AND broken** -- `$end` is an int, so `strtotime($end)` is `false` and `date('j', false)` is `'1'`, making it 1 on every day of the year, and it is never read. Deleted, not corrected. **THE FIX:** `App\Support\Reports\CalendarPeriod` derives the bounds, the label and the day count from ONE expression (`'first day of last month'`, which sets the day to 1 before subtracting, so there is nothing to overflow), with an **injectable clock** -- the change that makes the defect testable at all, since the bug appears on 33 days in five years, `Carbon::setTestNow()` does not move `date()`/`strtotime()`, and this container has no `faketime`. All seven call sites now read it; the three correct-but-separate `subMonthNoOverflow()` expressions are REMOVED rather than kept beside the fix, because a second expression is how these drifted. **MEASURED EQUIVALENT on all 1,827 days of 2024-2028: 0 disagreements between the old loop-bound expression and the new day count, so the 1,794 ordinary days are byte-identical.** **EVIDENCE:** `CalendarPeriodTest` 9 tests / 88 assertions over 1,827 days, expectations spelled from year-and-month arithmetic rather than date functions so they can disagree with the implementation; `LastMonthWindowOverflowTest` 7 tests driving a real overflow day through the two injectable seams, plus a comment-stripped SOURCE census that is the pin for the four in-handler sites the suite cannot execute (36 `MONTH(`/9 `YEAR(` in `selectRaw`, which SQLite lacks); `verify-gap223-lastmonth-window-on-mysql.php` 17 checks on MariaDB 10.11, showing the old bounds selecting **March's 3,000.00 across 2 orders under a card labelled February 2026** while February's 700.00 across 3 orders was absent. 14 mutations: **13 CAUGHT**, 1 EQUIVALENT and declared -- removing the `copy()` before `setDay()` is behaviourally identical because `setDay()` is absolute, measured in three months, so no test can distinguish it and none claims to. Unit 5,987 + 1 declared skip; Analytics/Merchant/report-scope 1,267; Reports 111. **NOT FIXED, and recorded as new leads rather than silently swept in:** the seven-day limb of `order_reports_chart` appends `$TotalOrderCount[]` TWICE per iteration (`:876`, `:890`), shipping a live series/label length mismatch of its own; and several dead assignments found in passing (`$TotalOrder` at `:1189` overwritten at `:1201`; `$customer`, `$customer_total`, `$totaluser`, `$guest` assigned in three branches and never read). Separate defects, separate slices. **(b) UNCHANGED AND OWNER'S:** whether a platform operator's pure READ of a tenant's figures falls under decision 7, whose text names money *operations*. `salesCollectionTile()` still refuses that principal; the six demand tiles still show store 1's order value. Narrowing them is a change to a live operator screen and is not engineering's to decide. |
| `GAP-224` | `MKT-002`, `GOV` | cross-phase | **A vendored module's Arabic catalogue can define the same JSON key as a first-party string, and 1,126 of them disagree with ours.** MEASURED 2026-11-21: `resources/lang/ar.json` holds **4,305** keys; the **68** `packages/workdo/*/src/resources/lang/ar.json` files redefine **1,589** of them -- **463** identically and **1,126 with different Arabic**. Worst offenders: `AuctionProduct` 102, `Marketing` 79, `LotteryPrizes` 78, `SidebarCustomization` 59, `PartialPayments` 51, `LynomiaChat` 47. The module side reads as machine-translated and is frequently **wrong in sense, not just in style**: `"Print"` -> `مطبعة` (*a printing press*), `"Type"` -> `يكتب` (*he types* -- the verb sense of an English noun label), `"Create"` -> `يخلق`, `"Delete"` -> `يمسح`, `"Search..."` -> `يبحث`, all third-person verb forms where Arabic UI convention takes the verbal noun. **PRECEDENCE IS MEASURED AND FAVOURABLE: the app catalogue wins.** Probed through a booted container for eight contested keys -- all eight resolved to *our* wording, so the 1,126 disagreements are **inert wherever `resources/lang/ar.json` defines the key**. Only **two** module JSON paths are registered in a no-addon boot (`LandingPage`, `LynomiaChat`); the other 66 load only with an `add_on_managers` row. The exposure is therefore **keys only a module defines**: `LynomiaChat` carries **753** such keys and `LandingPage` **5**, and those render unconditionally today. Confidence: certain (catalogue diff plus a runtime probe). | **HALF CLOSED 2026-11-21; the rest is a scoped translation review, not an engineering defect.** Found because mutation L1 -- deleting `"Running now"` from the app catalogue -- **SURVIVED** `MarketingFilterBarTest::test_every_option_wording_has_an_arabic_entry`: the assertion went through `Lang::get(..., 'ar')`, and `LynomiaChat`'s `قيد التشغيل الآن` answered in place of the deleted `جارٍ الآن`, so the test could not fail for **any** key a module also carries. **Both** Arabic checks in that test now read `resources/lang/ar.json` **directly** and assert a *first-party* translation exists; L1 re-run is **CAUGHT** (1 failed -> restore -> 1 passed). All **15** MKT-002 option wordings are present first-party, so this slice's own surface is clean. **NOT attempted, deliberately:** retranslating 1,126 shadowed keys or the 758 module-only ones is a bilingual content review, it would mean inventing wording for vendored modules this project must not edit outside `LynomiaChat`, and `Marketing`'s 79 overlap the Phase 05 surfaces -- so it is **owner scope**, not engineering. **Next action:** owner decides whether to commission an Arabic review of (a) the 758 keys only `LynomiaChat`/`LandingPage` define, which render today, and (b) the 1,126 shadowed keys, which render only if the app entry is ever removed. Engineering can add a CI census pinning the 1,589 overlap so a new disagreement is seen. |
| `GAP-225` | `ANA-009` | 05 | **The threshold alert is written and never delivered, and trend/anomaly/forecast detection is still absent — both are what keeps `ANA-009` PARTIAL after the threshold half shipped (2026-11-21).** Two distinct halves, recorded separately because they have different owners. **(a) DELIVERY.** `StoreNotificationService::metricThresholdBreached()` writes a `store_notifications` row with `push_sent => false` and sends nothing — no e-mail, no push. A merchant who does not open the bell is never told their figure fell below the floor they set, which is most of the value of an alert. This is a deliberate scope boundary rather than an oversight: the sibling types in that service push through Firebase, and wiring a NEW outbound message class into the consent and send-gate work (D8, enforcement still off by owner decision) is a decision this slice did not take. `MetricThresholdAlertTest::test_the_alert_is_not_pushed_and_that_is_the_contract` pins the current state so it cannot drift silently in either direction. **(b) TREND, ANOMALY AND FORECAST.** What shipped is a FLOOR: "this period's figure is below the number you typed". A trend needs a baseline, an anomaly needs a notion of normal, a forecast needs a model — each is a business policy, and the requirement row's own measurement names inventing one as the trap to avoid. Confidence: certain (both measured in the delivered code, neither inferred). | **OWNER, both halves, and they are separable.** (a) Delivery: the owner decides whether a threshold alert is a transactional message (so it may send while D8 marketing enforcement is off) or must wait for the consent framework to be active; engineering can then wire it to the existing Firebase path and/or the e-mail seam in a bounded slice. (b) Trend/anomaly/forecast: the owner supplies the comparison rule — baseline period, tolerance, and whether seasonality matters — or rules it out of scope. **Engineering can do neither without that.** What engineering has already done without a decision: the comparison, the configuration surface, the schedule and the deduplication, so only the policy and the channel remain. |
| `GAP-226` | `MKT-007`, `MKT-008`, `D8` | 05 | **The recovery pipeline detects, deduplicates and queues — and sends nothing, by owner decision.** MKT-007's engineering is complete through the queue: `marketing:detect-recovery-candidates` finds abandoned baskets and incomplete payments hourly, writes one row per subject, and records `send_state = 'held'` with the gate that held it. **No shopper is ever messaged by it.** Two switches are off and each is somebody else's decision: `config('marketing_consent.enforcement')` is `off` (D8 / MKT-008 — consent enforcement awaits owner approval and a human security review), and `config('marketing.nudge_min_interval_seconds')` is null (GAP-219 — how often a shopper may be messaged is a marketing policy). A source test asserts the sweep references no sender at all, so the state cannot drift silently in either direction. Confidence: certain (measured in the delivered code; the two config values are the shipped defaults). | **OWNER, and it is one decision with two parts.** (1) Is a recovery nudge a TRANSACTIONAL message (so it may send once consent enforcement is on) or a PROMOTIONAL one (so it also needs the cadence)? (2) When sending is approved, HOW is the existing backlog released — all at once, only candidates newer than a date, or not at all? The queue carries `detected_at` precisely so that this is a choice rather than an accident: on a store with a year of history, releasing everything would message every basket ever abandoned. **Engineering has nothing further to do here without (1).** What it can do on a yes, in a bounded slice: wire the queue's release to the existing `AbandonedMessageDispatch` and `NudgeLedger::reserve()` path — which already has the duplicate guard and the per-channel gate — and add a merchant-visible release control with whatever age rule the owner sets. |
| `GAP-227` | `ANA-004`, `ANA-007`, `GOV` | 05 | **`ANA-004` had no gap-register row at all, while every neighbouring `ANA` requirement has one — so nothing measured about conversion, device or channel analysis was tracked anywhere.** Found by the 49-agent read-only measurement of 2026-11-22, which also refuted the `ANA-004` traceability row itself: it recorded conversion analysis as "absent entirely" on a grep for `conversion_rate|conversionRate|funnel`, and the method is called `conversion()`. **(a) BUILDABLE NOW, NO OWNER DECISION NEEDED — four items.** (1) `orders.order_source` is a real sales-channel column with a closed three-value vocabulary (`pos|manual|storefront`) and a composite index `(store_id, order_source)`, merchant-visible as a Source column and filterable — and it is **never aggregated anywhere**: no "orders by channel" figure, chart or breakdown exists on any surface. Aggregating an indexed, closed-vocabulary column that the product already labels is engineering, not policy. (2) The conversion tile carries **no baseline and no delta**, although BOTH of its inputs (orders, page views) already carry one through the same `comparison` mechanism. (3) Visits have **no coverage-start object**, although the money tiles on the same screen disclose theirs through `LedgerCoverage` — so a merchant comparing a window that predates visit logging is shown a conversion rate with an understated denominator and no disclosure, which is the `ANA-007` failure mode the project has already fixed twice for money. (4) **UTM values are already retained verbatim** in `shetabit_visits` (`SanitisedVisitor` keeps the query string on both `url()` and `referer()`), and nothing parses them — so campaign attribution of PAGE VIEWS needs a reader, not a migration. **(b) NEEDS AN OWNER DECISION — three items, named so they are not built by default.** (1) A **device-CLASS rollup** (mobile / tablet / desktop) requires mapping 148 detector rule keys onto classes, which is a product judgement about what a merchant means by "mobile". (2) **Bot and crawler exclusion** requires deciding which agents are not customers; today none are excluded and the screen DISCLOSES that rather than guessing. (3) A **funnel** requires both new event capture (no table records an add-to-cart or a checkout attempt, and `carts` deletes the rows that converted) and a definition of what the stages are. **(c) STRUCTURALLY BLOCKED, recorded so it is not re-measured each time:** there is **no link of any kind between a visit and an order**, so conversion can never be segmented by device, channel, product or landing page until one exists. Every per-device or per-channel conversion figure depends on this one fact. **(d) NOT A DEFECT, recorded to stop a future census "fixing" it:** the referer breakdown is deliberately labelled *Referring pages* and never *channel*, and a missing referer is labelled *No referring page recorded* rather than *Direct* — because calling an absent referer "Direct" is a claim the data does not support. **(e) ALL FOUR OF (a) ARE NOW BUILT — and three defects were found IN THAT WORK, by an independent review run after the first two had already passed CI. Recorded because the pattern, not the individual bugs, is the lesson: every test asserted the returned ARRAY and none asserted the rendered PAGE or the EARLIER window.** (e1) **The conversion baseline (a)(2) was computed and shown to nobody.** `analytics/index.blade.php` passed `:value`, `:period` and `:hint` to the Conversion tile and not `:comparison`, unlike the two tiles above it — so the figure shipped in `62427391` with a green required run (#584) and reached no merchant. This is the same *shipped into a slot nobody could read* failure `ss.stat`'s own docblock records for hints. Fixed, with an assertion against the rendered screen rather than the array. (e2) **The coverage disclosure (a)(3) covered only the CHOSEN window.** A window can start after coverage — and so be rightly silent — while the equally long baseline window STRADDLES the coverage start, making its count a real non-zero number taken over part of a window. The delta then reports a trend that did not happen. The two visit-derived comparisons are now withheld in that case, with a sentence saying why; the WHOLLY-uncovered case keeps `comparison()`'s deliberate zero-baseline answer, because a first version of the guard fired there too and reddened seven tests that encode it. (e3) **The disclosure's wording was wrong about its own date.** It said "Page views for this store begin on X". Visit rows carried **no `store_id` at all** until the attribution fix of 2026-11-20, so `MIN(created_at)` is the day attribution began, not the day logging did — and the sentence will fire on nearly every store for the first weeks after deploy. Reworded to "Page views are recorded against this store from X … earlier visits were not attributed to any store", which is what the data supports. The same measurement refuted a paragraph in `MerchantAnalyticsFigures`'s own docblock claiming `shetabit_visits` holds the whole history; it is corrected in place. **(f) STILL OPEN after (e), carried rather than closed:** `visitCoverageStart()` runs `MIN(created_at)` over a store's entire visit history, unbounded, on every analytics render, export and scheduled send; `shetabit_visits` is indexed on `store_id` alone. It is behind `Manage Reports` so it is not an unauthenticated amplifier, and the composite-index remedy is already recorded against the referrer breakdown as an owner-level migration decision — this adds one more query to that set. **2026-11-25 — ITEM (a)(1) IS BUILT AND A LIVE DEFECT IN IT WAS FOUND AND FIXED.** The MySQL harness found, on its first run, that three orders whose `order_source` reads `POS`, `pos`, `pos` form ONE case-insensitive group on MariaDB while the screen relabels every one of them *"POS (not a source this version knows)"* — **with the count still correct**, so the breakdown sums to the order total and looks healthy. One row carrying a spelling the writers never produce (a restored dump, a hand edit, a future writer — all three anticipated in that method's own comment) captures the whole group. **SQLite's BINARY collation makes it two groups and hides the defect entirely**, which is why only a production-engine harness could find it. 3 of 5 failed first on SQLite; 5 pass now; 12 MySQL checks 0 failed with the fix and **2 FAILED with it reverted**, printing the real mislabel; 5 mutations all caught. Fixed by folding the bucket key BEFORE THE LABEL LOOKUP ONLY, so the display keeps the first spelling seen and `sources()`'s written promise that an unknown value appears "with the stored value" still holds. No rule invented: the vocabulary is three lower-case constants, `orderSourceLabel()` is a `match` on exactly those, the `trim()` beside it already existed for the same reason, and the Top Sales payment-method chart already groups case-insensitively with this argument in its own comment. **Items (a)2–4 were already built** — verified, not assumed. **(b) and (f) remain the owner's.** |
| `GAP-228` | `ANA-006`, `P4` | cross-phase | **A MERCHANT CANNOT EVER ADD A MISSING PRODUCT COST, so the "estimated profit" disclosure can never be driven to zero by any action available to them.** MEASURED 2026-11-22 by direct read, found while re-measuring `ANA-006`. `ProductController::update()` (`app/Http/Controllers/ProductController.php:1714`) **validates** `cost_price` as `required|numeric|min:0|lte:price` at `:1869`, with its own custom messages at `:1892-1893` — and **never assigns it**. The only `cost_price` write in the entire 5,000-line file is `:837`, inside `store()`'s simple-product branch. `cost_price` is **not** in `Product::$fillable`, so mass assignment does not save it either. **CONSEQUENCE, stated as what a merchant experiences:** a product sitting at a cost of 0 stays at 0 for ever. The merchant opens the edit form, the form *demands* a cost price and refuses to submit without one, they type it, the save succeeds — and the value is discarded in silence. **TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE FIRST DRAFT OF THIS ROW, both found while building the fix and both recorded rather than quietly amended.** (1) `products.cost_price` is **NOT NULL**, so "no cost on record" means **0**, not NULL — a first-draft test tried to write NULL and SQLite refused it. (2) The first draft said this made the "at least N paid orders have no product cost on record" disclosure irreducible. **That was too strong.** `store_order_financials.product_cost` is a SNAPSHOT taken at order time and the disclosure counts rows where that snapshot is 0, so fixing the product does **not** change orders already placed — nor should it, because those are historical financial records and rewriting them would be a financial modification the owner reserves. **WHAT THE DEFECT ACTUALLY COSTS, corrected:** it makes the problem PERPETUAL rather than irreducible. A product stuck at 0 snapshots 0 onto **every future order**, so N grows for ever and the merchant has no way to stop it. The fix stops the propagation; it does not and must not rewrite history. **WHY IT IS A CORRECTNESS DEFECT AND NOT A DISCLOSURE ONE:** `ANA-006` is satisfied by disclosing the omission, and that disclosure is correct. This is the separate fact that **the omission is permanent by construction**, which no amount of honest labelling fixes. **FIXED 2026-11-22.** `$product->cost_price = $request->cost_price;` assigned in `update()`'s SIMPLE-PRODUCT branch, beside `price` and `sale_price`, exactly mirroring `store():837`. **No coalesce, deliberately:** `sale_price` needs `?? 0` because it is validated `nullable` against a NOT NULL column; `cost_price` is validated `required` on this branch, and defaulting it to 0 would be worse than failing because **0 is a meaningful cost** ("this costs me nothing") and must not double as "they sent nothing". **The variant branch is deliberately untouched:** `cost_price` is in neither the variant rule set nor `store()`'s variant path, and a variant parent carries `price = 0` because the money lives per variant — giving it a cost would invent a figure. Evidence: 4 tests (3 failed before the fix), **4 mutations, 4 caught**, including the `?:` coalesce that would have swallowed an explicit 0 and the removal of `lte:price`. **NOT a policy decision** — no threshold, rate or business rule is involved; the form already demands the value and the model already has the column. |
| `GAP-229` | `GOV`, `MKT-001`, `MKT-010`, `ANA-002` | governance | **EIGHT OF NINE Phase 05 traceability rows were wrong about the code on 2026-11-22, and three of them were wrong in the direction that causes DUPLICATE WORK.** Corrected in the same change; recorded here because the pattern is the defect, not the nine rows. **THE PATTERN: a census searches for an identifier the feature does not use, and its zero result is written into the matrix as an absence.** Four instances are now measured: (1) `ANA-004` searched `conversion_rate|conversionRate|funnel`; the method is `conversion()` and it ships on five merchant surfaces. (2) `MKT-001` searched **`theme_analytic`** — a different route (`ThemeAnalyticController`, uri `themean-alytic`) from `analytics.index`; all five Marketing screens link to analytics through a shared partial. (3) `ANA-002` searched `type="date"` inside `resources/views/reports/*.blade.php`; the input lives in the shared component `components/ss/report-filter.blade.php:69`, six report screens render it, **and `ReportFilterBarTest::test_each_report_screen_uses_the_shared_bar` already passes on exactly those six** — the row contradicted a passing Feature test in this repository. (4) `MKT-002` searched five disclosure literals; all five Marketing surfaces carry a sixth mechanism (`x-ss.marketing-filters`). That row's verdict survived, but only because the disclosure is phone-only — which the grep could not have told anyone either way. **A FIFTH, DIFFERENT FAILURE: `MKT-010`'s defect was fixed and closed as `GAP-220` on 2026-11-21 and the matrix row was never updated**, so a closed gap kept reading as open for a day. That is a write-back failure, not a search failure, and it needs a different guard. **THE RULE THIS ESTABLISHES, now applied:** an absence claim must be established by BEHAVIOUR — find the surface a merchant would use and read what it renders, read the layer that feeds it, and only then search by name, for several plausible names — and must state which searches were run. A zero grep result is evidence about a string, never about a capability. **NOT a code change.** Carried as a governance gap so the next census is written differently. |
| `GAP-233` | `P04-P1-06`, `P4-F002` | 04 | **`product-detail` AND `product-detail-guest` DISCLOSE THE WHOLE FOREIGN PRODUCT ROW, PLUS REVIEWER NAMES AND E-MAIL ADDRESSES — strictly worse than the `cart-check-guest` leak closed the same day, which disclosed one integer.** Found by the independent tenant-authz review of the GAP-131 fix, which was asked for the siblings precisely so closing one route could not read as closing the family. `app/Http/Controllers/Api/ApiController.php:5711` `Product::where('id', $id)->first()` with no store predicate, serialised wholesale into `product_info` at `:5713-5714` (price, sale_price, product_stock, stock_status, description, detail, cover image); `:5718` returns the foreign product's `ProductImage` gallery; `:5726` its `Testimonial` rows, whose `user_name` and **`user_email`** are emitted at `:5753-5754`. `Testimonial` is not in `scoped_models` either. **WHOSE e-mail, corrected against the model rather than taken from the review:** `Testimonial::UserData()` is `hasOne(User::class, 'id', 'user_id')`, so the address is a **`users.email`** — another MERCHANT's or their staff's account address, reached through `testimonials.user_id` (added later, by `2024_03_19_104643_add_user_id_column_in_testimonials_table.php`; the table has no customer FK at all). Routes `routes/api.php:101` and `:102`, both `custom.auth`, so any live Sanctum token of any store — customer, merchant or driver — reaches them. Only `releted_products` (`:5797`) and `product_instruction` (`:5805`) are store-scoped. **A ROBUSTNESS DEFECT IN THE SAME HANDLER:** `product_detail_guest` has **no `empty($store)` check** at `:5818`, unlike every sibling, so an unknown slug reaches `Product::instruction_array($store->id)` at `:5893` and dereferences null — HTTP 500. Confidence: certain, live, read end to end. | Scope all three reads to the slug-resolved store, add the missing `empty($store)` guard, and drive it failing-first with a customer, merchant AND driver token — a customer-token probe alone proves nothing on this family. Use the shape GAP-131 settled: gate a dependent lookup on the already store-verified parent rather than adding a second `store_id` predicate, because `product_variants.store_id` and `testimonials.store_id` carry the same NULL/drift exposure recorded in `GAP-237`. |
| `GAP-234` | `P04-P1-06` | 04 | **`sub-category` and `sub-category-guest` return EVERY STORE'S UNCATEGORISED PRODUCTS when `category_id` is omitted.** `app/Http/Controllers/Api/ApiController.php:7130` and `:7192` are `Product::where('category_id', $request->category_id)->where('product_type', null)->paginate(10)` with no store predicate, reached through the `else` of `if (Category::find($request->category_id))` (`:7089` / `:7151`; `Category` is not in `scoped_models`). Post the route with **no `category_id`**: `Category::find(null)` is null, the else branch runs, Illuminate converts `where('category_id', null)` to `whereNull('category_id')`, and `products.category_id` is nullable (`2023_12_26_092822_create_products_table.php:19`) — so the response is a paginated page of every tenant's uncategorised products. (c) **Two live 500s beside it** — **AND THE LINES THIS ROW NAMED WERE THE WRONG ONES, which is the more useful finding.** The row recorded `$productStock_data->stock` (`:6825`) and `$product_data->name` (`:6826`, `:6831`). | `GAP-236` | `GOV`, `GAP-229` | governance | **THE GUARD-CLASS CENSUS HAS A THIRD SYNTAX IT DOES NOT SEARCH FOR, so its zero results understate the family.** CLAUDE.md records the productive defect pattern as `if (! auth('customers')->user()) { $id = $request->some_id; }` — a shape reachable by a merchant's or driver's token because `CustomAuth` logs a token's owner into the guard matching its tokenable class. The independent review of the GAP-131 fix found that **`auth('customers')->guest()` is the same test written the other way round** and that a grep for `! auth('customers')->user()` misses it: three further sites at `ApiController:5028`, `:5370`, `:10023`, plus `payment_list:2299`, which the review read end to end and found contained (its data source is `Cart::cart_list_cookie()`, which carries `->where('store_id', $store_id)`). This is `GAP-229`'s pattern — a census keyed on one spelling — in a family where the project believed it had an inventory. **AND THE BLIND SPOT IS AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE LARGER THAN THE REVIEW'S THREE SITES. Counted over `app/` on 2026-11-25:**

| spelling | occurrences in `app/` |
|---|---|
| `! auth('customers')->user()` — **the one the project documents** | **12** |
| `!auth('customers')->user()` — the same test without the space | **43** |
| `auth('customers')->check()` | 26 |
| `auth('customers')->guest()` | 9 |
| `Auth::guard('customers')` | 75 |

**165 occurrences, of which the documented spelling reaches 12 — and the no-space variant alone is more than three times that.** These are RAW OCCURRENCES, not defect sites: the shape is only dangerous where the else-branch derives an identifier from REQUEST INPUT, and the review proved `payment_list:2299` contained. So the figure is not 165 defects; it is 165 statements of which an unknown number are, and the project's inventory covers 12. That distinction is the finding. | Triage the 165 by WHAT THE BRANCH DOES, not by spelling: a site is a defect only where the non-customer branch takes an identifier from request input. Work outward from the 43 no-space sites, which nothing has ever looked at. Then pin the SPELLING SET itself in a census test, so a sixth syntax reddens the build instead of silently shrinking the inventory — whitespace-insensitive matching, since one space is what hid 43 sites. Do NOT report a count of occurrences as a count of defects; that is the error this row exists to stop. |
| `GAP-237` | `P04-P1-06`, `D-20` | 04 | **`product_variants.store_id` IS NULLABLE AND HAS BEEN MEASURED DRIFTING, AND THE APPLICATION HOLDS TWO CONTRADICTORY POLICIES ABOUT WHAT THAT MEANS.** `ProductController:2873-2895` records the measurement: an operator's ordinary no-op save stamped `store_id = 1` on a **store-7** product's variants while `products.store_id` stayed 7, because the upsert resolves by `product_id`+`variant` with no store predicate and `getCurrentStore()` answers the literal 1 for an operator with no selected store. That comment names the readers which then "stop seeing them" — the D-20 detection query, `Api\DashboardController::UpdateVariant`'s `where('store_id', $store->id)`, and the store teardown's `ProductVariant::where('store_id', …)->delete()`, so **a drifted row survives the deletion of its own store**. It self-heals only on the next save of that combination. NULL is reachable too: both writers go through `getCurrentStore()`, which answers null for a storeless user, and `ProductController:2941-2942` records that the `choice_no` path creates such rows. **THE POLICY DISAGREEMENT:** `productOfCartLineStore()` (`ApiController:6346-6373`) deliberately **accepts** a NULL `store_id` and refuses "only a provable mismatch", and the shipping quote uses it — while every reader above refuses NULL outright. So a basket holding an orphaned product gets a shipping quote and a "Product no found." stock check. The GAP-131 fix avoided becoming a fourth blind reader by gating the variant lookup on the already store-verified **product** instead of on the column; that is a workaround for one handler, not a decision about the column. Confidence: the writers, the drift and the disagreement are **certain** (code plus this project's own measured comments); how many live rows are affected is **unmeasured**. | **Measure first, with one query:** `select count(*) from product_variants v join products p on p.id = v.product_id where v.store_id is null or v.store_id <> p.store_id`. Then settle the policy ONCE — is a NULL or drifted `store_id` a provable mismatch or not? — and apply it to every reader, so `cart_check_guest` and `productOfCartLineStore` stop disagreeing. Backfilling the column from `products.store_id` is a data repair on a live table and therefore **the owner's**, not engineering's. |

| `GAP-252` | `P03-D-03`, `ARCH` | 03 | **VERIFIED COMPLETE (fixed 2026-11-25). 41% OF ARABIC HEADING IDS NAMED A WORD THAT DOES NOT EXIST, and it was invisible by construction.** `DocumentationRenderer::slug()` replaced `[^\p{L}\p{N}]+` with `-`. **An Arabic diacritic is Unicode category `Mn` (mark), which matches NEITHER `\p{L}` nor `\p{N}`**, so every shadda, fatha and tanween became a hyphen INSIDE a word: `سجّل` -> `سج-ل`, `أكّد بريدك الإلكتروني` -> `أك-د-بريدك-الإلكتروني`, `ما الذي تحدّده الباقة` -> `ما-الذي-تحد-ده-الباقة`. **24 of the 58 Arabic headings — 41% — produced an id naming a non-word.** WHY NOBODY SAW IT: the id and the table-of-contents link come from the SAME function, so every anchor resolved correctly and the page worked; the only symptom was a mangled fragment in the address bar, and the renderer's own docblock promised the opposite behaviour using an example with no diacritics. Found while closing `GAP-106` (LTR code isolation), not by looking for it. | **DONE** — `\p{M}` joined the kept classes. 14 of 29 failed first; 29 pass now; 2 mutations caught, breaking 4 of 7 unit cases and 10 of 21 corpus cases. **Blast radius measured rather than assumed:** the corpus has ZERO body links (the same measurement that shaped `GAP-109`'s validator), so nothing points at any of these fragments and no redirect is owed. **The reusable lesson is the detection problem, not the regex:** a slug function that feeds both the anchor and the link to it cannot be caught by navigating the page — only by reading the id against the heading it claims to name.
