| `GAP-261` | `P04-P6-04`, `FIN` | 04 | **OPEN. A ZERO-VALUE RETURNED LINE PLUS A FLAT COUPON RAISES THE ORDER TOTAL.** Pre-existing, adjacent to the GAP-140 edit but untouched by it, and found by its review. In `Order::product_return()` a line a refund has already emptied contributes **0** to `$return_price`; the flat-coupon subtraction then makes that total **negative**, and `final_price = original_total - (-c)` **increases** `orders.final_price` by the coupon amount. It settles after one such call, because the later recomputation applies the same coupon to the prior set. Reachable by the owning customer, an assigned driver, a merchant holding `Manage Order`, or the platform operator. **It harms the CUSTOMER, not the merchant** — on a COD order the figure is the cash collected at the door — which is the opposite direction from most findings in this family and the reason it is recorded separately rather than as a footnote. The existing "names no line" guard does not cover it, because a line **does** match. | **Engineering, in `Order::product_return()`'s money half, which GAP-140 deliberately did not touch.** The shape is to clamp the returned total at zero before the coupon is applied, or to skip the coupon adjustment when no line contributed value — but which one is a pricing question, so measure both against a real multi-line order with a flat coupon before choosing. Needs a failing test first: the figures are reachable in the suite, since this is host code rather than a module. **FIXED 2026-11-25, AND THE PRICING QUESTION WAS SETTLED BY MEASURING BOTH SHAPES.** Failing first, driven directly against `Order::product_return()` on a two-line cod order (a refund-emptied line, a 40.00 line, 17.50 of tax, total 57.50) with a 10.00 FLAT coupon: `orders.final_price` went 57.50 -> **67.50** and `orders.return_price` was written **-10.00**; `returnedAmountOf()` answered **-10.00** for the same line. The register's note that it settles was confirmed -- the case that returns the second, real line afterwards PASSED before the fix, because the inflated figure washes out of the next call's reconstructed base -- which is why the harm is the window, not the end state. BOTH CANDIDATE SHAPES WERE APPLIED AND RUN. *Skip the coupon when no line contributed value* closes the zero-line extreme and leaves the family open: a 5.00 line returned under the same 10.00 coupon still writes `5.00 - 10.00` and still raises the amount owed, 62.50 -> **67.50**, because a line DID contribute, just less than the coupon (1 of 12 cases still red). *Clamp the returned total at zero* closes both (28 of 28 with `SecondReturnTotalTest`). **The clamp was chosen**, as `max(0.0, $return_price - $coupon_price)`, applied to BOTH copies of the formula -- the live summation and `returnedAmountOf()`, which reconstructs the base and would otherwise understate the original order. It is the shape this codebase already uses one door along: `OrderRefundController`'s settlement writes `max(0.0, $grandTotal)` and records that PRORATING a flat coupon across returned lines is a revenue-reporting decision rather than something to guess at. What the clamp decides, stated rather than left to be found: a return worth less than the flat coupon now credits the customer NOTHING instead of charging them more. 12 new cases, 5 red first, 5 mutants all caught. It does NOT repair rows already inflated; that needs a production read and stays an owner item. **AND ONE RESIDUAL, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED, WITH A DIFFERENT CAUSE: `orders.final_price` can still end NEGATIVE.** When the order's stored `coupon_price` exceeds what the detail row's `coupon_discount_number` takes off the returned total, the subtraction still overshoots: measured on 90.00 + 40.00 of goods with `coupon_price` 100.00 and a detail row whose configured number is 0.00, returning both lines writes `final_price` **-100.00** with `return_price` 130.00 -- identical before and after the clamp, because the clamp only floors a NEGATIVE returned total and this one is positive. That is the recorded-versus-configured coupon disagreement the P4-F134/F135 notes left open (`orders.coupon_price` holds the coupon's CONFIGURED number while the child row holds what was charged), and flooring `final_price` here would hide it rather than fix it -- the same reason the tax column in this method is left untouched instead of guessed at. Reported, not repaired. |
| `GAP-262` | `P04-P8-02`, `FIN`, `I18N` | 04 | **OPEN, AND IT CHARGES THE MERCHANT. A SHIPPING METHOD THE MERCHANT NAMED THEMSELVES SHIPS FREE.** Found while seeding the P4-8 fixture, and verified by changing nothing but the name. `CartController::get_shipping_method` selects the cost arm by comparing `shipping_methods.method_name` — **free text the merchant types into their own form** — against three hardcoded English strings (`Delivery Syria`, `Flat Rate`, `Local pickup`); every other name falls to an `else` that sets the cost to **0**. Measured on one row, same `cost` column, same zone, same jurisdiction, only the name changed: `Local pickup` -> `shipping_final_price` **"$25.00"**; `استلام من المتجر` (the Arabic for the same thing) -> **"$0.00"**. **So a merchant who names a method `Standard`, `Express`, or anything at all in Arabic, ships for free and absorbs the carrier's cost** — on a product whose premise is bilingual operation, naming your shipping method in your own language is the trigger. The structured columns that should decide this (`calculation_type`, `shipping_requires`) are read only INSIDE the `Flat Rate` arm, so they never get a say for any other name. | **Engineering is obvious and the DECISION is not, so it is carried as `OWNER-DECISION-PACKAGE.md` I13.** The fix is to decide the arm from `calculation_type`/`shipping_requires` rather than from the name — but every merchant currently shipping free by accident would start charging, which is a live, shopper-visible price change, and the standing escalation rules reserve prices to the owner. **Pinned meanwhile:** `ShippingAndTaxReconciliationTest::test_a_shipping_method_the_merchant_named_themselves_ships_free` asserts the present behaviour with a control proving the same row charges correctly under a name the handler knows, so the decision shows up as a failing test rather than as silence. Invert that case when I13 is taken; do not relax it. |
| `GAP-263` | `P04-P5-01`, `FIN` | 04 | **OPEN, LIVE, AND IT DECIDES WHEN A MERCHANT GETS PAID. EVERY ORDER TAKEN OVER TELEGRAM SITS UNPAID.** Found by the GAP-136 gateway census and verified here by reading the constant and the route. `PaymentVerificationService::OFFLINE_PAYMENT_TYPES` means "settles off-platform by arrangement, so record Paid at once" and lists `bank_transfer`, `whatsapp`, `pos` and `lottery product` — **not `telegram`**. But `POST {storeSlug?}/telegram` routes to `PaymentController::whatsapp`, the **same handler**: both are relay channels with no payment API and identical semantics. Measured by execution: `whatsapp` resolves to **Paid**, `telegram` to **Pending**. **The asymmetry was invisible until the owner's no-unverified-Paid decision inverted the default** — before it, every unenforced type was Paid, so the missing entry cost nothing. The constant's own docblock records that `pos` and `lottery product` were added *for exactly this reason*; `telegram` was missed in that same sweep. Nothing in the suite pinned it before GAP-136's ratchet. | **Owner decision, carried as `OWNER-DECISION-PACKAGE.md` I15 — the engineering is one list entry and the decision is not.** Payment status governs when the merchant's wallet is credited and when the order is treated as settled, so adding `telegram` changes a money rule for every store taking orders that way; the standing rules reserve that. Two readings are defensible and the owner must pick: (a) `telegram` is a relay exactly like `whatsapp` and was simply missed — add it, which is what the identical handler argues; or (b) the carve-out list is deliberately narrow and `telegram` should stay Pending until a merchant confirms collection, in which case `whatsapp`'s own entry is the one to re-examine. **Do not read this row as a recommendation for (a).** Pinned meanwhile by GAP-136's ratchet, so the decision shows as a failing test. |

Not changed here: the current behaviour is strictly better than the 500 it replaced, and changing it touches what every merchant sees on their first screen. **The test half needed no decision and is done:** the three cases now use `makeTenant()`, a provisioned active merchant is measured at HTTP 200 with no `Location`, and both directions of the predicate mutation are CAUGHT.
